(1.15)

Here we have only kept track of terms that are either not classically well-defined or contain stochastic diagrams which require construction. The final term involving
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
arises from applying commutators to the paraproduct term in the expansion of
\rho^{\delta}
where the more regular parts have been left implicit above. Since we only expect to have
\rho^{\delta}\rightarrow\rho
in
C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-1-}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
we do not expect (
1.15
) to converge directly. We have already identified the possibly diverging field which renormalises the first term, since

\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}=\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\in C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2}).

As discussed in the introduction, formal power counting would lead one to expect
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
to diverge at order
\delta^{-1}
, however, exploiting the symmetry of the elliptic Green’s function we have that
\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{0-}}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}
.

The diverging diagram
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
is also contained in the terms
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}
and
\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
. However, since
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
is of regularity
0-
and
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
of regularity
-1-
, it is not directly clear how to make sense of those products. Note that if
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
were a diverging constant rather than a field this would simply be scalar multiplication and we would have no trouble. Nevertheless, using that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
is deterministic, one can define the products
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}
and
\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
directly as Itô objects and show that they diverge at a rate no worse than
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
. We refer to Subsection
2.6
for this argument.

Since
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}=\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
, we may expand the product
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}}\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
to cancel the diverging terms
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}
and
\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
in (
1.15
). Hence, we can construct the renormalised product
\lim_{\delta\to 0}(\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\rho^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\rho^{\delta}}]-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})
without further modifications. In particular, this identifies the renormalising sequence
(f^{\delta})_{\delta>0}
of (
1.5
) as
f^{\delta}=\mathbb{E}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]
and (
1.14
) as the corresponding mild formulation.

We have therefore identified both the solution
\rho
and the non-linear term in (
1.10
) as trilinear functions of a suitable enhancement of the noise. To conclude this section we paraphrase the main result of this paper (Theorem
3.22
). In the following,
(\alpha,p,q,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\#},\beta_{0},\kappa,\eta)
denotes a tuple of exponents which determines the regularity and integrability of each object in our paracontrolled decomposition. We assume that those exponents satisfy assumption (
3.1
) in Section
3
, see also Examples
3.1
–
3.3
for various special cases of interest.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
(\alpha,p,q,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\#},\beta_{0},\kappa,\eta)
be a tuple of exponents satisfying (
3.1
),
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
be some initial data,
T>0
be a time horizon,
\bm{\xi}=(\xi^{1},\xi^{2})
be a vector-valued space-time white noise on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
be a heterogeneity and
(\psi_{\delta})_{\delta>0}
be a family of symmetric mollifiers. Then there exist enhancements
\mathbb{X}=(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}})
,
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}=(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})
as described above (in particular
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}
is built from
\sigma\bm{\xi}^{\delta}
with
\bm{\xi}^{\delta}=\psi_{\delta}\ast\bm{\xi}
) and a random variable
T_{\textnormal{max}}\in(0,T]
(the maximal time of existence) with the following properties:

1.

There exists a unique paracontrolled solution
\rho
to (
1.1
) on
[0,T_{\textnormal{max}})
with enhancement
\mathbb{X}
and initial data
\rho_{0}
(in the sense of Definition
3.10
), which we call the renormalised solution. The maximal time of existence
T_{\textnormal{max}}
satisfies

T_{\textnormal{max}}=T\qquad\text{or}\qquad\lim_{t\uparrow T_{\textnormal{max}}}\lVert(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\rho(t)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}=\infty\qquad\text{almost surely}.

2.

For each
\lambda>0
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sum_{L=1}^{\infty}\frac{2^{-L}}{L}\frac{\lVert\rho-\rho^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T_{L}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}}{1+\lVert\rho-\rho^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T_{L}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}}>\lambda\Bigr)=0,

where
\rho^{\delta}
is the solution to (
1.14
) and

T_{L}\coloneq T\wedge L\wedge\inf\Bigl\{t\in[0,T]:\lVert(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\rho(t)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-1-}}>L\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \lVert(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\rho^{\delta}(t)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-1-}}>L\Bigr\}

for every
L\in\mathbb{N}
.

Furthermore
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}=\mathbb{E}[\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]]
. If
\sigma
is a constant then
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\equiv 0
while in general one has the bound
\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}
(see Remark
2.29
and Lemma
2.27
).

Remark 1.3
.

In the case of constant
\sigma
it also holds that
\mathbb{E}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]=0
. This is due to the symmetry of the elliptic Green’s function, see the discussion of (
1.6
).

Remark 1.4
.

Since the submission of this manuscript it has been brought to our attention that in the particular case of the Keller–Segel non-linearity
\rho\nabla\Phi_{\rho}
, one may in fact simplify the analysis of the equation (
1.10
) in the following manner: Using that
\Phi
denotes the resolution of the mean-free Laplacian on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, one may cast the Keller–Segel non-linearity in the following form

\rho\nabla\Phi_{\rho}=-\Delta\Phi_{\rho}\nabla\Phi_{\rho}+\langle\rho,1\rangle_{L^{2}}\nabla\Phi_{\rho},

where
the first term can be written by the product rule as

\begin{split}\Delta\Phi_{\rho}\nabla\Phi_{\rho}=\nabla\cdot(\nabla\Phi_{\rho})^{\otimes 2}-\frac{1}{2}\nabla\trace(\nabla\Phi_{\rho})^{\otimes 2},\end{split}

with

(\nabla\Phi_{\rho})^{\otimes 2}_{i,j}\coloneq(\nabla\Phi_{\rho}\otimes\nabla\Phi_{\rho})_{i,j}\coloneq\partial_{i}\Phi_{\rho}\partial_{j}\Phi_{\rho},\qquad i,j=1,2.

Hence, we may re-write (
1.10
) as

\begin{split}\rho&=P\rho_{0}-\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\nabla\cdot(\nabla\Phi_{\rho})^{\otimes 2}]+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\nabla\trace(\nabla\Phi_{\rho})^{\otimes 2}]+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\langle\rho,1\rangle_{L^{2}}\nabla\Phi_{\rho}]+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}.\end{split}

(1.16)

Using the bilinearity of each operator on the right-hand side of (
1.16
), it follows that the Da Prato–Debussche remainder
u=\rho-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}
satisfies the equation

u=P\rho_{0}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}]+\hat{Q}(u,\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}),

where
\hat{Q}(u,\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}})
denotes a finite sum of classically well-posed terms involving
\nabla\Phi_{u}
and
\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}
rather than
u
and
. Consequently, it suffices to renormalise the product
\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}]
by subtracting
, without the need to perform a paracontrolled decomposition. Nevertheless our methods are still of independent interest, as they demonstrate how one may construct a noise enhancement that involves heterogeneities and anisotropic regularities. We thank M. Gubinelli and H. Weber for these insights.

2
Noise Enhancement

In this section we construct the enhancements required in Theorem
1.2
and establish their regularities.

2.1
Outline and Regularities

We begin by defining a vector
\bm{\xi}=(\xi^{1},\xi^{2})
of space-time white noises as in
[
MWX17
]
. Let
(W^{j}(\cdot,m))_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},j=1,2}
be a family of complex-valued Brownian motions on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
starting from
0
that satisfy
\overline{W^{j}(\cdot,m)}=W^{j}(\cdot,-m)
and are otherwise independent.
We define for
j=1,2
, the space-time white noise
\xi^{j}
by setting for any
\phi\in L^{2}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{C})
,

\xi^{j}(\phi)\coloneq\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j}(u_{1},m_{1})\widehat{\phi}(u_{1},-m_{1}).

(2.1)

We define our choice of mollifiers.

Definition 2.1
.

We define the cut-off

\begin{split}&\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\penalty\ \text{to be of compact support},\penalty\ \text{supp}(\varphi)\subset B(0,1),\penalty\ \text{even}\penalty\ \text{and such that}\penalty\ \varphi(0)=1.\end{split}

(2.2)

Given
\varphi
satisfying (
2.2
), we define a sequence of mollifiers
(\psi_{\delta})_{\delta>0}
on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
by

\psi_{\delta}(x)\coloneq\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle\omega,x\rangle}\varphi(\delta\omega).

(2.3)

Our enhancements will lie in the following space of enhanced rough noises, which quantifies their regularities in interpolation spaces (see Section
1.1
and Definition
A.3
).

Definition 2.2

(Enhanced rough noise)
.

For all
T>0
,
\alpha\in(-5/2,-2)
and
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
let the map

\begin{split}\Theta&\colon(\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+5}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}))\\
&\to\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{3\alpha+6}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}),\\
(v,f)&\mapsto\Theta(v,f),\end{split}

be given by

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[{v}\nabla\Phi_{v}]-f,

\displaystyle\Theta(v,f)

\displaystyle\coloneq(v,Y,Y\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{v}+\nabla\Phi_{Y}\mathbin{\varodot}v,\nabla\mathcal{I}[v]\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{v}+\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{v}]\mathbin{\varodot}{v})

and define the space
\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
to be the closure of the subset

\begin{split}&\{\Theta(v,f):(v,f)\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+5}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}),\penalty\ v_{0}=f_{0}=0\}\\
&\qquad\subset\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{3\alpha+6}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}).\end{split}

We shall denote a generic element of this closure by
\mathbb{X}=(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}})\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and equip it with the metric induced by the norm

\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}\coloneq\max\{\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}},\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}},\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{3\alpha+6}},\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\}.

The main result of this section is the following theorem, reminiscent of
[
GP17
, Thm. 9.1]
.

Theorem 2.3
.

Let
T>0
,
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
,
\bm{\xi}
be a two-dimensional vector of space-time white noises,
(\psi_{\delta})_{\delta>0}
be a sequence of mollifiers as in (
2.3
),
\bm{\xi}^{\delta}\coloneq\psi_{\delta}\ast\bm{\xi}\coloneq(\psi_{\delta}\ast\xi^{1},\psi_{\delta}\ast\xi^{2})
and
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}\coloneq(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})
be given by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\sigma\bm{\xi}^{\delta}],\quad\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]-\mathbb{E}[\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]],

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\coloneq\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\quad\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\coloneq\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}]\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}+\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}.

Then for all
\alpha\in(-5/2,-2)
and
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
the following hold

1.

Almost surely,
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and

\mathbb{X}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+5}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{3\alpha+8}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})\times\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+6}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}).

2.

Almost surely, there exists some
\mathbb{X}=(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}})\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
such that for any
p\in[1,\infty)
we have
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathbb{X}-\mathbb{X}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}^{p}]^{1/p}=0
and
\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}^{p}]^{1/p}<\infty
.

3.

Defining

\begin{split}&\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\coloneq\mathbb{E}[\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]],\qquad\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]=\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\\
&\hskip 30.00005pt\ignorespaces\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}\coloneq\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}}\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\end{split}

it holds that

\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}

and for any
p\in[1,\infty)
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2},\\
&\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{3\alpha+6}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{3}.\end{split}

An explicit definition of the limit
\mathbb{X}=(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}})
can be found in Subsection
2.2
. We call
\mathbb{X}
the
renormalised enhancement
and
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}=(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})

.

).

Remark 2.4
.

Different aspects of Theorem
2.3
require different assumptions on the heterogeneity
\sigma
. For example the regularity of
and
only requires
\sigma\in C_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
(Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
2.17
) while the regularities of the contractions contained in
,
require that uniformly over
t\in[0,T]
one has
\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}|\widehat{\sigma}(t,\omega)|(1+|\omega|^{2})<\infty
. The assumption
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
implies both of these conditions and provides a convenient norm and well-studied space that controls the latter quantity; hence we choose to work with this simpler, if sub-optimal restriction. Furthermore, with a view to setting
\sigma=\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}
(cf. (
1.3
)) the condition
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
is more straightforward to check.

Remark 2.5
.

As discussed in the introduction, we build our
regular
enhancement from
\sigma\bm{\xi}^{\delta}=\sigma(\psi_{\delta}\ast\bm{\xi})
instead of
(\sigma\bm{\xi})^{\delta}=\psi_{\delta}\ast(\sigma\bm{\xi})
and with only a spatial convolution. Hence, for
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
to be more regular, we need to assume some regularity on the heterogeneity
\sigma
. We again make use of the condition
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(t,\omega)\rvert(1+\lvert\omega\rvert^{2})<\infty
(see the proof of Lemma
2.7
), though other choices are possible.

Remark 2.6
.

Our methods also allow us to establish that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\in\mathscr{L}_{T}^{1-}\mathcal{C}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. However, since we do not make use of the additional time regularity, we omit the proof.

In the remainder of this section, we outline the basic arguments involved in proving Theorem
2.3
. We motivate the definition of
and establish its existence.

It is well-known that the Fourier frequencies of the stochastic heat equation are given by Ornstein–Uhlenbeck processes and by a similar argument, we can find an expression for the Fourier transform of
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\sigma\bm{\xi}]
. For all
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, let
H_{t}^{j}(\omega)\coloneq 2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\omega^{j}\exp(-t\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})\mathds{1}_{t\geq 0}
be the multiplier appearing in
\partial_{j}\mathcal{I}
. We define
by applying the inverse Fourier transform to the sequence

\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}(t,\omega)\coloneq\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{2}\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j_{1}}(u_{1},m_{1})\widehat{\sigma}(u_{1},\omega-m_{1})H_{t-u_{1}}^{j_{1}}(\omega).

(2.4)

We also introduce the Fourier transform of
\tau\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\bm{\xi}]
by

\widehat{\tau}(t,\omega)\coloneq\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j_{1}}(u_{1},\omega)H_{t-u_{1}}^{j_{1}}(\omega).

(2.5)

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
T>0
,
\alpha<-2
,
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
and
\sigma\in C_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Then for any
p\in[1,\infty)
we have
\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}
and in particular
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
a.s. Assume in addition
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and
\delta>0
, then it holds that
\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim(1+\delta^{-2})^{1/2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}
and in particular
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
a.s. What is more,
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}^{p}]^{1/p}=0
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
.

Proof.

Let
\gamma\in(0,1]
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma/2)
and
\max\{1/\varepsilon,2\}<p<\infty
. To establish the existence and regularity of
in a Besov space, we apply Nelson’s estimate (Lemma
2.11
), Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion (Lemma
2.16
) and the Besov embedding (
A.1
). Therefore, in order to establish that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\in C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-1-\gamma-\varepsilon-2/p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\hookrightarrow C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-1-\gamma-3\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely, it suffices to show that the quantity

\sup_{s\neq t\in[0,T]}\lvert t-s\rvert^{-p\gamma/2}\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}}2^{pq(-1-\gamma-\varepsilon)}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,x)-\Delta_{q}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,x)\rvert^{2}]^{p/2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mux

is finite. Using that
\sigma\in C_{T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
is bounded, we can pass to real space with (
2.10
) to deduce for
\tau
as in (
2.5
),

\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,x)-\Delta_{q}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,x)\rvert^{2}]\leq\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\tau(t,x)-\Delta_{q}\tau(s,x)\rvert^{2}].

Using that
\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}(t,\omega)\overline{\widehat{\tau}(s,\omega^{\prime})}]=0
if
\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\tau(t,x)-\Delta_{q}\tau(s,x)\rvert^{2}]\\
&=\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle\omega,x\rangle}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle\omega^{\prime},x\rangle}\varrho_{q}(\omega)\varrho_{q}(\omega^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{\tau}(t,\omega)-\widehat{\tau}(s,\omega))\overline{(\widehat{\tau}(t,\omega^{\prime})-\widehat{\tau}(s,\omega^{\prime}))}]\\
&=\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)^{2}\mathbb{E}[\lvert\widehat{\tau}(t,\omega)-\widehat{\tau}(s,\omega)\rvert^{2}].\end{split}

It follows by Itô’s isometry and interpolation (
A.2
),

\mathbb{E}[\lvert\widehat{\tau}(t,\omega)-\widehat{\tau}(s,\omega)\rvert^{2}]\leq\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu_{1}\lvert H^{j_{1}}_{t-u_{1}}(\omega)-H^{j_{1}}_{s-u_{1}}(\omega)\rvert^{2}\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega\rvert^{2\gamma}

and therefore uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\tau(t,x)-\Delta_{q}\tau(s,x)\rvert^{2}]\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}2^{q(2+2\gamma)}.

To summarize, we obtain by Lemma
2.11
, Lemma
2.16
and the arguments above for each
\max\{1/\varepsilon,2\}<p<\infty
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-1-\gamma-3\varepsilon}}^{p}]\\
&\lesssim\sup_{s\neq t\in[0,T]}\lvert t-s\rvert^{-p\gamma/2}\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}}2^{pq(-1-\gamma-\varepsilon)}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,x)-\Delta_{q}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,x)\rvert^{2}]^{p/2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mux\\
&\leq\sup_{s\neq t\in[0,T]}\lvert t-s\rvert^{-p\gamma/2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{p}\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}}2^{pq(-1-\gamma-\varepsilon)}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\tau(t,x)-\Delta_{q}\tau(s,x)\rvert^{2}]^{p/2}\\
&\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{p}\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}}2^{-pq\varepsilon}\\
&\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{p},\end{split}

(2.6)

which we can generalize to
p\in[1,\infty)
by Hölder’s inequality. In particular it follows by Lemma
2.16
that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\in C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-1-\gamma-3\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely, where
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma/2)
can be chosen arbitrarily small.

Next we show that the approximating sequence
(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})_{\delta>0}
is more regular if
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. In Fourier space,

\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)=\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{2}\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j_{1}}(u_{1},m_{1})\widehat{\sigma}(u_{1},\omega-m_{1})\varphi(\delta m_{1})H_{t-u_{1}}^{j_{1}}(\omega).

(2.7)

We apply Itô’s isometry, the triangle inequality and interpolation (
A.2
) to estimate

\begin{split}&\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)-\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(s,\omega)\Bigr)\overline{\Bigl(\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega^{\prime})-\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(s,\omega^{\prime})\Bigr)}\Bigr]\Bigr\rvert\\
&\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{\gamma}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert\omega-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega^{\prime}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\delta m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1},\end{split}

where we used that
\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega)\rvert\lesssim(1+\lvert\omega\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}
uniformly in
u\in[0,T]
and
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, and that
(1+x^{2})^{1/2}\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
(the latter of which we leave implicit.)
We may assume
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
, since
\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,0)=0
. We decompose the sum over
m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
into the domains
m_{1}=0
,
m_{1}=\omega
,
m_{1}=\omega^{\prime}
and
m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert\omega-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega^{\prime}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\delta m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\\
&\leq\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2}+\delta^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2}+\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2})\\
&\quad+\delta^{-2}\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}}\lvert\omega-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert m_{1}\rvert^{-2}.\end{split}

We estimate the sum over
m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}
. Assume
\omega=\omega^{\prime}
, then by Lemma
C.5
and (
C.3
),

\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}}\lvert\omega-m_{1}\rvert^{-4}\lvert m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2}.

Assume
\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}
, we apply Lemma
C.4
to estimate for
\varepsilon<1
,

\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}}\lvert\omega-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lesssim\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2+\varepsilon}(\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+2\varepsilon}+\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2+2\varepsilon}).

Having established the decay of the Fourier coefficients, we can bound the Littlewood–Paley blocks. Let
q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}
, we obtain uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}(t,x)-\Delta_{q}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}(s,x)\rvert^{2}]&\leq\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)\varrho_{q}(\omega^{\prime})\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)-\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(s,\omega)\Bigr)\overline{\Bigl(\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega^{\prime})-\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(s,\omega^{\prime})\Bigr)}\Bigr]\Bigr\rvert\\
&\lesssim(1+\delta^{-2})\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}2^{q(2\gamma+3\varepsilon)}.\end{split}

Consequently, by Lemma
2.11
and Lemma
2.16
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma/2)
,

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-\gamma-4\varepsilon}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim(1+\delta^{-2})^{1/2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}

and in particular,
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-\gamma-4\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely, where
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma/2)
can be chosen arbitrarily small.

To deduce
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}^{p}]^{1/p}=0
for all
p\in[1,\infty)
,
\alpha<-2
and
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
, let
\gamma\in(0,1]
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma/2)
,
\max\{1/\varepsilon,2\}<p<\infty
and
0<\vartheta^{\prime}<\vartheta\leq 1
. We use Nelson’s estimate (Lemma
2.11
) and Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion (Lemma
2.16
) to bound

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-1-\gamma-\vartheta-3\varepsilon}}^{p}]\\
&\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert t-s\rvert^{-2-p(\gamma/2-\varepsilon)}\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}}2^{pq(-1-\gamma-\vartheta-\varepsilon)}\\
&\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})(t,x)-\Delta_{q}(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})(s,x)\rvert^{2}]^{p/2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mux\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mut.\end{split}

We then use Lemma
2.14
to bound

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})(t,x)-\Delta_{q}(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})(s,x)\rvert^{2}]\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2\vartheta^{\prime}}\lVert 1-\varphi\rVert_{C^{1}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)^{2}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu_{1}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\vartheta}\lvert H_{t-u_{1}}^{j_{1}}(\omega)-H_{s-u_{1}}^{j_{1}}(\omega)\rvert^{2}.\end{split}

Using the same estimates as in the derivation of (
2.6
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})(t,x)-\Delta_{q}(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})(s,x)\rvert^{2}]\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2\vartheta^{\prime}}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lVert 1-\varphi\rVert_{C^{1}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)^{2}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\vartheta}\lvert\omega\rvert^{2\gamma}\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2\vartheta^{\prime}}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lVert 1-\varphi\rVert_{C^{1}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}2^{2q(1+\gamma+\vartheta)}.\end{split}

Therefore,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-1-\gamma-\vartheta-3\varepsilon}}^{p}]\\
&\lesssim\delta^{p\vartheta^{\prime}}\lVert 1-\varphi\rVert_{C^{1}}^{p}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{p}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert t-s\rvert^{-2+p\varepsilon}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mut\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}}2^{-pq\varepsilon}\\
&\lesssim\delta^{p\vartheta^{\prime}}\lVert 1-\varphi\rVert_{C^{1}}^{p}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{p},\end{split}

which implies
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-1-\gamma-\vartheta-3\varepsilon}}^{p}]=0
. An application of Hölder’s inequality allows us to deduce the convergence for all
p\in[1,\infty)
, where
\varepsilon
and
\vartheta
can be chosen arbitrarily small. This yields the claim.
∎

The convergence of the other approximations in
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}
is analogous. The only crucial observation here is that we can choose
\vartheta^{\prime}
and
\vartheta
arbitrarily small when applying Lemma
2.14
, which ensures that the various applications of Lemma
C.3
in the proof of Theorem
2.3
carry over. Hence, we will only show the existence of
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{can}}
, the second part of Theorem
2.3
, that is, the convergence
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}\to\mathbb{X}
, then follows as above.

2.2
Feynman Diagrams

As demonstrated by (
2.4
), we may construct objects derived from white noise as (iterated) stochastic integrals. However, as we continue to multiply terms to construct the enhancement of Theorem
2.3
, we need to apply Itô’s product rule to increasingly complicated expressions. To implement this procedure efficiently, we use an extension of a graphical representation that was developed by
[
MWX17
,
GP17
]
, which relates our stochastic objects to Feynman diagrams.

We make use of several types of vertices, which will be introduced throughout this subsection. A root

(t,\omega)

represents the argument
(t,\omega)
.
A circle

denotes an instance of stochastic integration in time against a two-dimensional Brownian field with heterogeneity
\sigma
. Graphically this integrator is given by

\hbox to56.18pt{\vbox to16.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.09023pt\lower-2.08601pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.26 0 C 2.26 1.25 1.25 2.26 0 2.26 C -1.25 2.26 -2.26 1.25 -2.26 0 C -2.26 -1.25 -1.25 -2.26 0 -2.26 C 1.25 -2.26 2.26 -1.25 2.26 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

where the placeholder
\ldots
stands for an integrand in
u_{1}
,
\omega_{1}
,
m_{1}
,
j_{1}
, which is to be determined from the remaining diagram.

Generally, circles are enumerated by
k\in\mathbb{N}
and equipped with tuples of dummy variables
(u_{k},\omega_{k},m_{k},j_{k})
, where
u_{k}\in(0,\infty)
,
\omega_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,
m_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
j_{k}=1,2
, which we point out explicitly for the reader’s convenience. We refer to the
\omega
,
\omega_{k}
as the
frequencies
and the
m_{k}
as the
modes
. We denote coordinates of those by superscripts.

Vertices are connected by different types of directed edges, i.e. arrows, that represent integrands. Black arrows

(u_{k},\omega_{k},m_{k},j_{k})
(t,\omega)

are associated to the integrand
H_{t-u_{k}}^{j_{k}}(\omega_{k})
, which is the Fourier multiplier appearing in
\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}
. Highlighted arrows

(u_{k},\omega_{k},m_{k},j_{k})
(t,\omega)
j
,
for
j=1,2
, are associated to
G^{j}(\omega_{k})H_{t-u_{k}}^{j_{k}}(\omega_{k})
, where

G^{j}(\omega_{k})\coloneq 2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\omega_{k}^{j}\lvert 2\pi\omega_{k}\rvert^{-2}\mathds{1}_{\omega_{k}\neq 0}

is the multiplier for
\partial_{j}\Phi
. The direction of an arrow indicates the smaller time variable
u_{k}
in the integration. Furthermore, if a single arrow emerges from the root

(t,\omega)

, we multiply the integrand by
\mathds{1}_{\omega=\omega_{1}}
.

2.4
) as
