We first show Lemma
2.25
. Let us focus on

, the derivation for

and

is similar, but easier.

Proof of Lemma
2.25
.

Let
s,t\in[0,T]
,
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
j=1,2
and
q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}
. An application of (
2.10
) yields

\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}]\leq\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}],

where

is defined by

\begin{split}&\widehat{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,\omega,j)\\
&\coloneq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\omega=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}+\omega_{4}\\
(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}=1}^{2}\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu_{3}\int_{0}^{u_{3}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu_{2}\int_{0}^{u_{3}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j_{1}}(u_{1},\omega_{1})\widehat{\sigma}(u_{2},\omega_{2}-m_{2})\widehat{\sigma}(u_{2},\omega_{4}+m_{2})\\
&\quad\times(G^{j}(\omega_{4})+G^{j}(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}))H^{j_{3}}_{t-u_{3}}(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})H^{j_{2}}_{t-u_{2}}(\omega_{4})G^{j_{3}}(\omega_{1})H^{j_{1}}_{u_{3}-u_{1}}(\omega_{1})H^{j_{2}}_{u_{3}-u_{2}}(\omega_{2}).\end{split}

Using the definition of the Littlewood–Paley block
\Delta_{q}
, uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}]\\
&\leq\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)\varrho_{q}(\omega^{\prime})\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\widehat{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,\omega,j)-\widehat{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,\omega,j)\Bigr)\overline{\Bigl(\widehat{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,\omega^{\prime},j)-\widehat{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,\omega^{\prime},j)\Bigr)}\Bigr]\Bigr\rvert.\end{split}

We apply Itô’s isometry and a decay estimate (Lemma
C.1
) for the symmetrized elliptic multiplier
G^{j}(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})+G^{j}(\omega_{4})=G^{j}(\omega-\omega_{4})-G^{j}(-\omega_{4})
. We obtain the bound

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}]\\
&\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)\varrho_{q}(\omega^{\prime})\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}+\omega_{4}\\
(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{2}^{\prime},\omega_{4}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega^{\prime}=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}^{\prime}+\omega_{4}^{\prime}\\
(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}^{\prime})\sim\omega_{4}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j_{3},j_{3}^{\prime}=1}^{2}\sum_{m_{2},m_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\\
&(1+\lvert\omega_{2}-m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{2}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{4}+m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{4}^{\prime}+m_{2}^{\prime}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\\
&\times\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}^{\prime}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1})(1+\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}^{\prime}\rvert^{-1})\mathsf{A}_{s,t}^{j_{3}}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\omega_{4},\omega_{2})\mathsf{A}_{s,t}^{j_{3}^{\prime}}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}^{\prime},\omega_{4}^{\prime},\omega_{2}^{\prime}).\end{split}

(2.20)

We control the shape coefficient with Lemma
B.2
and obtain for all
\gamma\in[0,1]
,

\mathsf{A}_{s,t}^{j_{3}}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\omega_{4},\omega_{2})\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{2\gamma}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-1}.

We can then plug this expression into (
2.20
) and apply Lemma
C.6
to control the sums over
\omega_{4}
and
m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Let
\gamma\in(0,1/2)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1-2\gamma)
, it follows that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}]\\
&\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\gamma}\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)\varrho_{q}(\omega^{\prime})\lvert\omega\rvert^{2\gamma+\varepsilon}\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{2\gamma+\varepsilon}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}(1\vee\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-2+\varepsilon}(1\vee\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-2+\varepsilon}.\end{split}

(2.21)

Hence it suffices to control the remaining sum over
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
,

\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}(1\vee\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-2+\varepsilon}(1\vee\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-2+\varepsilon}.

We distinguish the cases
\omega=\omega^{\prime}
and
\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}
. In the case
\omega=\omega^{\prime}
, we decompose the sum into the regions
\omega_{1}=\omega
and
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}
. We then estimate by Lemma
C.3
,

\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}(1\vee\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-4+2\varepsilon}=\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2}+\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-4+2\varepsilon}\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+2\varepsilon}.

In the case
\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}
, we decompose the sum into the regions
\omega_{1}=\omega
,
\omega_{1}=\omega^{\prime}
and
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}(1\vee\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-2+\varepsilon}(1\vee\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-2+\varepsilon}\\
&=(\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2}+\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2})\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2+\varepsilon}+\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2+\varepsilon}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2+\varepsilon}\end{split}

and apply Lemma
C.4
to bound

\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2+\varepsilon}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2+\varepsilon}\lesssim\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2+\varepsilon}\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+2\varepsilon}+\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2+\varepsilon}\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-2+2\varepsilon}.

Assume
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\text{supp}(\varrho_{q})
, it follows that
2^{q}\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert\lesssim 2^{q}
,
2^{q}\lesssim\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert\lesssim 2^{q}
and if
\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}
then
2^{q}\lesssim\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert\lesssim 2^{q}
as well. We obtain by (
2.21
),

\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}]\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\gamma}2^{q(4\gamma+5\varepsilon)}.

Assume in addition
\varepsilon<\gamma
, we obtain by Lemma
2.11
and Lemma
2.16
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
,

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\gamma-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-2\gamma-5\varepsilon}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}

and therefore
\raisebox{-3.59515pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
a.s. for any
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
.
∎

Next we prove the existence of

.

Proof of Lemma
2.26
.

Let
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
,
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
k,j=1,2
. We can bound the increment

\begin{split}&\lvert\widehat{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,\omega,k,j)-\widehat{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,\omega,k,j)\rvert\\
&\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\\
\omega_{1}\sim\omega_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\lvert G^{j}(\omega_{2})+G^{j}(\omega_{1})\rvert}{(1+\lvert\omega_{1}+m_{2}\rvert^{2})(1+\lvert\omega_{2}-m_{2}\rvert^{2})}\mathsf{A}_{s,t}^{k}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{1}).\end{split}

We control the shape coefficient with Lemma
B.2
and the elliptic multiplier with Lemma
C.1
. Let
\kappa\in[0,1]
, we arrive at

\begin{split}&\lvert\widehat{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,\omega,k,j)-\widehat{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,\omega,k,j)\rvert\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa}\lvert\omega\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\\
\omega_{1}\sim\omega_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-1})\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-3+2\kappa}}{(1+\lvert\omega_{1}+m_{2}\rvert^{2})(1+\lvert\omega_{2}-m_{2}\rvert^{2})}.\end{split}

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we bound by Lemma
C.3
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}+m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{2}-m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\\
&=2(1+\lvert\omega\rvert^{2})^{-1}+\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{-\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\}}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}+m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{2}-m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\\
&\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+2\varepsilon}\end{split}

and obtain

\lvert\widehat{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,\omega,k,j)-\widehat{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,\omega,k,j)\rvert\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa}\lvert\omega\rvert^{-1+2\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\\
\omega_{1}\sim\omega_{2}\end{subarray}}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-1})\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-3+2\kappa}.

For any
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
, we bound by Lemma
C.3
,

\lvert\widehat{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(t,\omega,k,j)-\widehat{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}(s,\omega,k,j)\rvert\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa}\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+2\kappa+2\varepsilon}.

It follows directly that

\lVert\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{-2\kappa-2\varepsilon}}\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}

and we obtain
\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\in\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
for any
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
.
∎

2.6
Construction of the Canonical Enhancement

In this section we construct
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
and
{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}
,
{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}
for correlation lengths
\delta>0
. Additionally, we bound their speeds of divergence as
\delta\to 0
by a logarithmic rate using the symmetry of the elliptic equation.

Lemma 2.27
.

Let
T>0
,
\alpha<-2
,
\kappa\in(0,1)
,
\delta>0
,
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and
\varphi
be as in (
2.2
). Then it holds that

\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2},\qquad\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+5}}\lesssim(1\vee(\delta^{-1}\log(\delta^{-1})))\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2},

and for any
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
,
p\in[1,\infty)
,

\mathbb{E}[\lVert{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{3\alpha+6}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2},

and

\mathbb{E}[\lVert{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{3\alpha+6}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}.

Proof.

We first establish
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}_{T}^{1-}\mathcal{C}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Let
s,t\in[0,T]
,
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
\kappa\in(0,1)
. It suffices to consider
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
, since
\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,0)=0
. We symmetrize the contraction. Changing the rôles of
\omega_{1}
,
\omega_{2}
in the definition of
\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)
and using that
\varphi
is even, we obtain

\begin{split}\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\omega=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{3}=1}^{2}\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu_{3}\int_{0}^{u_{3}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu_{1}\widehat{\sigma}(u_{1},\omega_{1}-m_{1})\widehat{\sigma}(u_{1},\omega_{2}+m_{1})\lvert\varphi(\delta m_{1})\rvert^{2}\\[-16.0pt]
&\hskip 170.00026pt\ignorespaces\times H^{j_{3}}_{t-u_{3}}(\omega)H^{j_{1}}_{u_{3}-u_{1}}(\omega_{1})H^{j_{1}}_{u_{3}-u_{1}}(\omega_{2})(G^{j_{3}}(\omega_{1})+G^{j_{3}}(\omega_{2})).\end{split}

We apply the triangle inequality, Lemma
C.1
and (
A.2
) to bound

\begin{split}&\lvert\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)-\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(s,\omega)\rvert\\
&\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa}\lvert\omega\rvert^{2\kappa}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}}\frac{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-1})}{(1+\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{2})}.\end{split}

We can now apply Lemma
C.7
to control the sums over
m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}
, and
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}
for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
,

\lvert\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)-\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(s,\omega)\rvert\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa}\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+2\kappa+3\varepsilon}

and so

\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{-3\varepsilon}}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}.

The proof that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{1-}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
with a divergence of order

follows by a similar derivation, where we skip the symmetrization and use that

.

Next we establish that
{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta},{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}_{T}^{1/2-}\mathcal{C}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
. We first consider
{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}
. We apply Itô’s isometry, (
2.11
) and Lemma
C.1
to bound uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}]\\
&\lesssim\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{6}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)\varrho_{q}(\omega^{\prime})\\
&\quad\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\\
(\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega-\omega_{4}=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}^{\prime},\omega_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}=\omega_{1}^{\prime}+\omega_{2}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j_{3},j_{3}^{\prime}=1}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\lvert m_{1}^{\prime}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\\
&\quad(1+\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{2}+m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}^{\prime}-m_{1}^{\prime}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{2}^{\prime}+m_{1}^{\prime}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}\\
&\quad\times\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}\rvert\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{2}^{\prime}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-1})(1+\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}^{\prime}\rvert^{-1})\\
&\quad\times\mathsf{A}_{s,t}^{j_{3},j_{3}^{\prime}}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\omega_{1}^{\prime}+\omega_{2}^{\prime},\omega_{4}).\end{split}

Assume
s\leq t
,
\gamma\in[0,1]
,
\delta>0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
whose range we continue to restrict throughout the proof. We apply Lemma
B.3
to control the shape coefficient and subsequently Lemma
C.7
to obtain

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}]\\
&\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))^{2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{6}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho(\omega)\varrho(\omega^{\prime})\\
&\quad\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\\
(\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}.\end{split}

Assume
6\varepsilon<4-2\gamma
, we apply Hölder’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\\
(\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}\\
&\leq\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-4+2\gamma+6\varepsilon}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega^{\prime}\}\\
(\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-4+2\gamma+6\varepsilon}\Bigr)^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim(1\vee\lvert\omega\rvert)^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}(1\vee\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert)^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon},\end{split}

(2.22)

which implies

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\lvert\Delta_{q}{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}(t,x,j)-\Delta_{q}{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}(s,x,j)\rvert^{2}]\\
&\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))^{2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{6}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varrho_{q}(\omega)\varrho_{q}(\omega^{\prime})(1\vee\lvert\omega\rvert)^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}(1\vee\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert)^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}.\end{split}

Assume in addition
\gamma\in(0,1)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma/2)
, we obtain by Lemma
2.11
and Lemma
2.16
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
,

\mathbb{E}[\lVert{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-\gamma-6\varepsilon}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}

and therefore
{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
a.s. for any
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
.

The only difference between
\widehat{{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}}
and
\widehat{{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}}
is that the factor
G^{j}(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})
replaces
G^{j}(\omega_{4})
. Instead of (
2.22
), we estimate by Hölder’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\\
(\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-3+\gamma+3\varepsilon}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-3+\gamma+3\varepsilon}\\
&\leq\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{\omega\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-6+2\gamma+6\varepsilon}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{\omega^{\prime}\}\\
(\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-6+2\gamma+6\varepsilon}\Bigr)^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim(1\vee\lvert\omega\rvert)^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}(1\vee\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert)^{-2+\gamma+3\varepsilon}.\end{split}

As before, we obtain by (
2.11
), Lemma
2.11
and Lemma
2.16
for any
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
6\varepsilon<4-2\gamma
,
\varepsilon<\gamma/2
,
\delta>0
and
p\in[1,\infty)
,

\mathbb{E}[\lVert{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}^{\gamma/2-\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{-\gamma-6\varepsilon}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1}))\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{3}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}

and therefore
{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
a.s. for any
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
.
∎

Remark 2.28
.

We may also construct
\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,^{\delta}=\mathbb{E}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}]
, which is similar to
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
but without the lower stem (cf. (
2.8
)). However, to obtain
\kappa
-time regularity, we need to trade
2\kappa
-space regularity in the parabolic multipliers
H_{t-u_{1}}^{j_{1}}(\omega_{1})H_{t-u_{1}}^{j_{1}}(\omega_{2})
. We then sum over
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, hence we will be stuck with a divergence of
\delta^{-2\kappa}
, where
\kappa
is arbitrarily small but positive.

Remark 2.29
.

We can show that
\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,^{\delta}\equiv 0
, if
\sigma\equiv 1
. Indeed, if we choose
\sigma\equiv 1
, then
\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega)=\mathds{1}_{\omega=0}
. Consequently on the right-hand side of (
2.8
),
\omega_{1}=m_{1}
and
\omega_{2}=-m_{1}
. By the symmetrization, we obtain the factor
G^{j}(\omega_{1})+G^{j}(\omega_{2})
. Using that
G^{j}
is odd and that
\omega_{1}=-\omega_{2}
, we see that this term is zero, so that
\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,^{\delta}\equiv 0
. Similarly,
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}={\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}={\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}\equiv 0
, if
\sigma\equiv 1
.

3
Existence of Paracontrolled Solutions

In this section we show the existence and uniqueness of a paracontrolled solution to (
1.1
) given an abstract enhancement, where the notion of paracontrolled solution has previously been motivated in (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) and will be made precise in Definition
3.10
.

In Subsection
3.1
we consider the solution on small time intervals (Lemma
3.12
), which we then extend in Subsection
3.2
to a maximal time of existence (Lemma
3.20
). In Subsection
3.3
we then combine the deterministic solution theory above with the stochastic existence of the renormalised enhancement (Theorem
2.3
) to construct the renormalised solution to (
1.1
) as a random variable (Theorem
3.22
, Part
1
). We can then show that solutions to (
1.14
) converge in probability to the renormalised solution (Theorem
3.22
, Part
2
).

3.1
Local Well-Posedness

Throughout we fix exponents satisfying the assumptions below. To explain their usage:
p
and
\beta_{0}
will be the integrability and regularity exponents of the admissible initial condition in the Besov scale
\mathcal{B}^{\beta_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, where
q
is the microscopic parameter;
\alpha
will be the regularity of the space-time white noise, so that almost surely
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\in C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
;
\beta
measures the regularity of the second Da Prato–Debussche remainder,
w
, in the Hölder scale and
\eta
the allowed blow-up of
w
at
t=0
;
\beta^{\prime}
measures the regularity of the Gubinelli derivative
w^{\prime}
;
\beta^{\#}
measures the maximal spatial regularity of the paracontrolled remainder and finally
\kappa
will be used to denote time regularity.

From now on we fix
(\alpha,p,q,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\#},\beta_{0},\kappa,\eta)
satisfying

\begin{split}\alpha\in(-9/4,-2),&\qquad q\in[1,\infty],\\
p\in(2/(\alpha+3),\infty],&\qquad\beta\in(-1/2,\alpha+2),\\
\beta^{\prime}\in(-2\alpha-4,(\beta+1)\wedge(2\alpha+5)],&\qquad\beta^{\#}\in(-\alpha-2,\alpha+\beta^{\prime}+2),\\
\beta_{0}\in(\beta+2/p-(\alpha+3),\beta^{\#}],&\qquad\kappa\in((\beta^{\#}-\alpha-2)/2,1/2),\\
\eta\in\Bigl[\Bigl(\Bigl(\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{2}\vee 0\Bigr)+\frac{1}{p}\Bigr)&\vee\Bigl(\frac{\beta^{\#}-\beta_{0}}{4}+\frac{1}{2p}\Bigr),\frac{\alpha+3}{2}\Bigr).\end{split}

(3.1)

One can confirm that the intervals in (
3.1
) are non-empty.

Particular choices of exponents allow us to consider regular and irregular initial data:

Example 3.1
.

By taking
p=\infty
,
\beta_{0}=\beta^{\#}
and
\eta=0
, we can choose as initial data any
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{\infty,q}^{\beta^{\#}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, without incurring a blow-up at
0
.

Example 3.2
.

By taking
p=q=\infty
and
\beta<2\alpha+4
,
we can choose as initial data any
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, which is the natural regularity of
and the state space of
\rho=\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+w
.

Example 3.3
.

Using that
L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}_{p,\infty}^{0}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
(see Subsection
A.1
), we can choose as initial data any
\rho_{0}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
with
p>2
.

Let us fix a
T>0
, we define the space of paracontrolled distributions.

Definition 3.4
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. We define the space

\mathscr{D}_{T}\subset\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})\times(\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\cap\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}))

of distributions paracontrolled by
\mathbb{X}
as those triples
\bm{w}\coloneq(w,w^{\prime},w^{\#})
such that

w=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+w^{\#}

(3.2)

as well as
\lim_{t\to 0}w_{t}=\lim_{t\to 0}w_{t}^{\#}=\rho_{0}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
and
\lim_{t\to 0}w^{\prime}_{t}=\nabla\Phi_{\rho_{0}}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
. We equip this space with the metric induced by the norm

\lVert\bm{w}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{T}}\coloneq\max\{\lVert w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}},\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\}.

Remark 3.5
.

The Ansatz (
3.2
) allows us to write the mild equation for
w^{\#}
as
w^{\#}=P\rho_{0}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})]
with some
\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})
determined by (
1.10
). We can hence simplify our estimates by using the space-time regularization of
\mathcal{I}
. Note that (
3.2
) is equivalent to the Ansatz (
1.11
) discussed in the introduction, up to commutators.

Lemma 3.6
.

Given
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, the space
\mathscr{D}_{T}
is a non-empty, complete metric space.

Proof.

To show that
\mathscr{D}_{T}
is non-empty, we can choose
w^{\prime}=\nabla\Phi_{P\rho_{0}}
,
w^{\#}=P\rho_{0}
and subsequently set
w\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+w^{\#}
. The initial condition is satisfied, since
\lim_{t\to 0}w^{\prime}_{t}=\nabla\Phi_{\rho_{0}}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\lim_{t\to 0}w^{\#}_{t}=\rho_{0}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
. By Lemma
A.6
and Lemma
A.8
, using that
\beta^{\prime}\leq\beta+1
, and
((\beta-\beta_{0})/2\vee 0)+1/p\leq\eta
, we obtain
w^{\prime}=\nabla\Phi_{P\rho_{0}}\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
w^{\#}=P\rho_{0}\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
and

\lVert\nabla\Phi_{P\rho_{0}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lVert\nabla\Phi_{P\rho_{0}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta+1}}\lesssim\lVert P\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}}.

Using that
\beta_{0}\leq\beta^{\#}
and
(\beta^{\#}-\beta_{0})/2+1/p\leq 2\eta
, we obtain
w^{\#}\in\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
and

\lVert P\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}}.

Since
w=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+w^{\#}
, we find by an application of the triangle inequality, Lemma
A.6
and Lemma
A.4
, that
w\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
and

\lVert w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},

where we used that
\beta<\alpha+2
and
\beta^{\prime}>0
.
To show completeness, let
(\bm{w}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a Cauchy sequence in
\mathscr{D}_{T}
. By the completeness of
\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\times\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})\times(\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})\cap\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}))
, we obtain
w_{n}\to w\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
,
w^{\prime}_{n}\to w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
w^{\#}_{n}\to w^{\#}\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
and
w^{\#}_{n}\to w^{\#}\in\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
all with the correct initial conditions. It suffices to show
w=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+w^{\#}
so that
\bm{w}\in\mathscr{D}_{T}
. Then again by Lemma
A.6
and Lemma
A.4
,

w_{n}-\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]-w^{\#}=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[(w^{\prime}_{n}-w^{\prime})\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+w^{\#}_{n}-w^{\#}\to 0\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}),

which combined with
w_{n}\to w\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
yields
w=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+w^{\#}
.
∎

In the next lemma we show that
w\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{w}
is well-defined in
\mathscr{D}_{T}\times\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
.

Lemma 3.7
.

There exists a continuous operator
\mathscr{P}\colon\mathscr{D}_{T}\times\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}\to C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
, such that when all objects are smooth,

\mathscr{P}(\bm{w},\mathbb{X})=w\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{w}.

Proof.

Using the notation
\mathscr{C}(f,g,h)=(f\mathbin{\varolessthan}g)\mathbin{\varodot}h-f(g\mathbin{\varodot}h)
(see Lemma
A.11
) and recalling that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}=\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}+\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}]\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}
we can expand the product into

\begin{split}\mathscr{P}(\bm{w},\mathbb{X})&\coloneq\mathscr{C}(w^{\prime},\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}],\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}})+\mathscr{C}(w^{\prime},\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}],\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}})+(w^{\#}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]-w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}])\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\\
&\quad+(\nabla\Phi_{w^{\#}}+\nabla\nabla\cdot\Phi_{\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]}-w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}])\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+w^{\prime}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\end{split}

where
\bm{w}\in\mathscr{D}_{T}
and
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
. In what follows, we establish the bound

\lVert\mathscr{P}(\bm{w},\mathbb{X})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\lesssim_{T}(\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}+\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}})(1+\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}})^{2}

(3.3)

by various applications of our commutator results. The regularity
\mathscr{P}(\bm{w},\mathbb{X})\in C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
follows by the same arguments.

Using Lemma
A.11
and that
\beta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
,
2\alpha+4<0<2\alpha+4+\beta^{\prime}
, we obtain

\lVert\mathscr{C}(w^{\prime},\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}],\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lVert\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}}\lVert\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}}

and

\lVert\mathscr{C}(w^{\prime},\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}],\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lVert\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}]\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+3}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}.

Further, by Lemma
A.4
, using that
2\alpha+4<0<\beta^{\#}+\alpha+2
,

\lVert(w^{\#}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]-w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}])\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\lesssim\lVert w^{\#}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]-w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\lVert\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}}.

To control the remainder, we apply Lemma
A.9
and Lemma
A.10
, using that
\kappa\in((\beta^{\#}-\alpha-2)/2,1/2)
and
\beta^{\#}<\alpha+\beta^{\prime}+2
,

\lVert w^{\#}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]-w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}+\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}.

Similarly, by Lemma
A.4
, Lemma
A.9
and Lemma
A.10
, using that
2\alpha+4<0<\beta^{\#}+\alpha+2
,

\begin{split}&\lVert(\nabla\Phi_{w^{\#}}+\nabla\nabla\cdot\Phi_{\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]}-w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}])\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\\
&\lesssim_{T}(\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}+\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}})\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}.\end{split}

Finally, by Lemma
A.4
, using that
2\alpha+4<0<2\alpha+4+\beta^{\prime}
,

\lVert w^{\prime}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}.

This yields the claim.
∎

We can now derive a priori bounds for our solution map.

Lemma 3.8
.

For every
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, let
\bm{\Psi}
, acting on
\bm{u}=(u,u^{\prime},u^{\#})\in\mathscr{D}_{T}
, be given by
\bm{\Psi}(\bm{u})\coloneq(w,w^{\prime},w^{\#})
, where

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} w&\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+w^{\#},\quad w^{\prime}\coloneq\nabla\Phi_{u}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}},\\
w^{\#}&\coloneq P\rho_{0}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\Omega^{\#}(\bm{u})],\end{aligned}\end{cases}

and

\begin{split}\Omega^{\#}(\bm{u})&\coloneq u\nabla\Phi_{u}+u\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\nabla\Phi_{u}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\\
&\quad+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}+u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}u+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{u}+\mathscr{P}(\bm{u},\mathbb{X}).\end{split}

Then
\bm{\Psi}(\bm{u})\in\mathscr{D}_{T}
and there exists some
\theta>0
depending only on the chosen exponents and the dimension, such that for every
T\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\max\{\lVert w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+T^{\theta}\lVert\bm{u}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{T}})^{2}(1+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}+\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}})^{2},

(3.4)

\displaystyle\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}.

(3.5)

Proof.

We derive bounds for our solution map in several steps. By the same arguments it also follows that
\bm{\Psi}(\bm{u})\in\mathscr{D}_{T}
. In the remainder of the proof, we will assume
T\leq 1
.

Step
1
. The
\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
-regularity of
w
.
As in the proof of Lemma
3.6
, but this time keeping track of the dependency on
T
, we see that

\lVert w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim(T^{1-\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}}\vee T^{1-\kappa})\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

Step
2
. The
\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
and
\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
-regularity of
w^{\#}
.
To establish the
\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
-regularity of
w^{\#}
, we apply Lemma
A.6
, using that
((\beta-\beta_{0})/2\vee 0)+1/p\leq\eta
,
\eta<1/2
,
\beta+1<\alpha+\beta+4
and
-(\alpha+1)/2\vee\kappa<1-\eta
,

\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim T^{\eta-(\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{2}\vee 0)-\frac{1}{p}}\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}}+(T^{\frac{\alpha+3}{2}-\eta}\vee T^{1-\kappa-\eta})\lVert\Omega^{\#}(\bm{u})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}}.

To establish the
\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R})
-regularity of
w^{\#}
, we apply Lemma
A.6
as above, but this time using that
\beta_{0}\leq\beta^{\#}
,
(\beta^{\#}-\beta_{0})/2+1/p\leq 2\eta
,
\eta<1/2
,
\beta^{\#}+1<\alpha+\beta+4
and
(\beta^{\#}-\alpha-\beta-1)/2\vee\kappa<1
,

\lVert w^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\lesssim T^{2\eta-\frac{\beta^{\#}-\beta_{0}}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}}+(T^{1-\frac{\beta^{\#}-\alpha-\beta-1}{2}}\vee T^{1-\kappa})\lVert\Omega^{\#}(\bm{u})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}}.

Step
3
. The
C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
-regularity of
\Omega^{\#}(\bm{u})
.
We obtain by various applications of Lemma
A.4
, using in particular that
4\alpha+9>0
and
\beta>-1/2
,

\lVert u\nabla\Phi_{u}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}^{2},

\begin{split}\max\Bigl\{\lVert u\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},&\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\nabla\Phi_{u}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},\lVert u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},\\
&\lVert\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}u\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{u}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}}\Bigr\}\lesssim T^{\eta}\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\max\Bigl\{\lVert{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},&\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},\lVert\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},\\
&\lVert{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},\lVert{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}}\Bigr\}\lesssim T^{2\eta}(1+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}})^{2}.\end{split}

By (
3.3
) of Lemma
3.7
, using that
\alpha+\beta+2\leq 2\alpha+4
,

\lVert\mathscr{P}(\bm{u},\mathbb{X})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}}\lesssim(\lVert u^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}+\lVert u^{\#}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}})(1+\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}})^{2}.

Step
4
. The
\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
-regularity of
w^{\prime}
.
By definition,
w^{\prime}=\nabla\Phi_{u}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}
. Using that
\beta^{\prime}\leq\beta+1
and
\beta^{\prime}\leq 2\alpha+5
, we obtain

\lVert w^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+T^{\eta}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}.

Step
5
. Closing the bounds.
Using that
T\leq 1
, we can collect all of the terms above and cast them in the form (
3.4
)–(
3.5
). This yields the claim.
∎

While Lemma
3.8
shows that
\bm{\Psi}
is a map from
\mathscr{D}_{T}
to itself, it is not a contraction for small
T
, since there is no small time parameter on the right-hand side of (
3.5
). The remedy is to apply
\bm{\Psi}
twice and argue that a fixed point of
\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}
is also a fixed point of
\bm{\Psi}
itself.

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Then there exists some
\bar{T}\in(0,T\wedge 1]
such that there is a unique solution
\bm{w}=(w,w^{\prime},w^{\#})\in\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}
to the equation

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} w&=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+w^{\#},\quad w^{\prime}=\nabla\Phi_{w}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}},\\
w^{\#}&=P\rho_{0}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})].\end{aligned}\end{cases}

(3.6)

The time of existence
\bar{T}
depends only on
\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}
,
\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}}
, the chosen exponents and the dimension.

Proof.

We let
\bar{T}\in(0,T]
,
\bar{T}\leq 1
,
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
,
\bm{u}\in\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}
and define

(\Psi(\bm{u}),\Psi(\bm{u})^{\prime},\Psi(\bm{u})^{\#})\coloneq\bm{\Psi}(\bm{u}).

By Lemma
3.8
there exists some
\theta>0
such that

\max\{\lVert\Psi(\bm{u})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert\Psi(\bm{u})^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert\Psi(\bm{u})^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\}\lesssim(1+\bar{T}^{\theta}\lVert\bm{u}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}})^{2}(1+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{\bar{T}}}+\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}})^{2},

(3.7)

and

\lVert\Psi(\bm{u})^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{\bar{T}}}.

(3.8)

Now denote

(\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{u}),\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{u})^{\prime},\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{u})^{\#})\coloneq\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{u}).

By iterating the bounds (
3.7
)–(
3.8
), using
\bar{T}\leq 1
to streamline exponents, we obtain

\lVert\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{u})\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}\lesssim(1+\bar{T}^{\theta/2}\lVert\bm{u}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}})^{4}(1+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{\bar{T}}}+\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}})^{6}.

(3.9)

Let
C>0
be larger than the implicit constants of the inequalities (
3.7
) and (
3.9
) above. Assume that
M,R>0
are sufficiently large such that

(1+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}+\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}})<M,\qquad 2CM^{6}<R.

Assume further that
\lVert\bm{u}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}<R
. Using the bound (
3.9
), we can choose
\bar{T}=\bar{T}(R,\theta)\leq 1
smaller, if necessary, such that

\lVert\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{u})\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}\leq C(1+\bar{T}^{\theta/2}R)^{4}M^{6}\leq 2CM^{6}<R.

Consequently,
\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}
is a self-mapping on the ball

\mathfrak{B}_{R;\bar{T}}\coloneq\{\bm{u}\in\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}:\lVert\bm{u}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}<R\}.

Upon choosing
R>0
sufficiently large, we can ensure that
\mathfrak{B}_{R;\bar{T}}\subset\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}
is non-empty.

To achieve contractivity, we use the bilinearity of the equation. Let
\bm{v}=(v,v^{\prime},v^{\#}),\bm{w}=(w,w^{\prime},w^{\#})\in\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}
and denote

(\Psi(\bm{v}),\Psi(\bm{v})^{\prime},\Psi(\bm{v})^{\#})\coloneq\bm{\Psi}(\bm{v}),\qquad(\Psi(\bm{w}),\Psi(\bm{w})^{\prime},\Psi(\bm{w})^{\#})\coloneq\bm{\Psi}(\bm{w}).

We see that

\displaystyle\Psi(\bm{v})-\Psi(\bm{w})
