\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[(\Psi(\bm{v})^{\prime}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\prime})\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+\Psi(\bm{v})^{\#}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\#},

\displaystyle\Psi(\bm{v})^{\prime}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\nabla\Phi_{v-w},

\displaystyle\Psi(\bm{v})^{\#}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\#}

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\Omega^{\#}(\bm{v})-\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})],

where

\begin{split}\Omega^{\#}(\bm{v})-\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})&=v\nabla\Phi_{v-w}+(v-w)\nabla\Phi_{w}+(v-w)\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\nabla\Phi_{v-w}+(v-w)\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\\
&\quad+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}(v-w)+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{v-w}+\mathscr{P}(\bm{v},\mathbb{X})-\mathscr{P}(\bm{w},\mathbb{X}).\end{split}

The difference of the renormalised products is given by

\begin{split}\mathscr{P}(\bm{v},\mathbb{X})-\mathscr{P}(\bm{w},\mathbb{X})&=\mathscr{C}(v^{\prime}-w^{\prime},\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}],\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}})+\mathscr{C}(v^{\prime}-w^{\prime},\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}],\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}})\\
&\quad+(v^{\#}-w^{\#}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[(v^{\prime}-w^{\prime})\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]-(v^{\prime}-w^{\prime})\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}])\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}\\
&\quad+(\nabla\Phi_{v^{\#}-w^{\#}}+\nabla\nabla\cdot\Phi_{\mathcal{I}[(v^{\prime}-w^{\prime})\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]}-(v^{\prime}-w^{\prime})\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}])\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+(v^{\prime}-w^{\prime})\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}.\end{split}

Using the same bounds as before, we obtain, for some
\theta>0
,

\begin{split}&\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})-\Psi(\bm{w})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\bar{T}^{\theta}\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})^{\prime}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\prime}\rVert_{C_{\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})^{\#}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\end{split}

and

\max\{\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})^{\#}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})^{\#}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\}\lesssim\bar{T}^{\theta}\lVert\Omega^{\#}(\bm{v})-\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}},

as well as

\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})^{\prime}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lVert v-w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

For the right-hand side,

\begin{split}&\lVert\Omega^{\#}(\bm{v})-\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+2}}\\
&\lesssim\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lVert v-w\rVert_{C_{\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+\lVert v-w\rVert_{C_{\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lVert w\rVert_{C_{\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+\lVert v-w\rVert_{C_{\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\\
&\quad+\lVert v-w\rVert_{C_{\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\lVert\mathscr{P}(\bm{v},\mathbb{X})-\mathscr{P}(\bm{w},\mathbb{X})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}.\end{split}

By the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
3.7
we have

\begin{split}&\lVert\mathscr{P}(\bm{v},\mathbb{X})-\mathscr{P}(\bm{w},\mathbb{X})\rVert_{C_{2\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}}\\
&\lesssim(\lVert v^{\prime}-w^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}+\lVert v^{\#}-w^{\#}\rVert_{C_{2\eta;\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}})(1+\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\rVert_{C_{\bar{T}}\mathcal{C}^{2\alpha+4}})^{2}.\end{split}

Combining the bounds above, we obtain

\displaystyle\max\{\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})-\Psi(\bm{w})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})^{\#}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})^{\#}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\#}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}}\}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\bar{T}^{\theta}\lVert\bm{v}-\bm{w}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}(1+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{\bar{T}}}+\lVert v\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+\lVert w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}})^{2},

(3.10)

\displaystyle\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})^{\prime}-\Psi(\bm{w})^{\prime}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lVert v-w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

(3.11)

Next we consider

(\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{v}),\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{v})^{\prime},\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{v})^{\#})\coloneq\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{v}),\qquad(\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{w}),\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{w})^{\prime},\Psi^{\circ 2}(\bm{w})^{\#})\coloneq\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{w}).

Iterating (
3.10
)–(
3.11
), we arrive at

\begin{split}&\lVert\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{v})-\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{w})\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}\\
&\lesssim\bar{T}^{\theta}\lVert\bm{v}-\bm{w}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}(1+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{\bar{T}}}+\lVert v\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+\lVert w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}+\lVert\Psi(\bm{w})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}})^{4}.\end{split}

Assume
\bm{v},\bm{w}\in\mathfrak{B}_{R;\bar{T}}
, it follows by (
3.7
), the definition of
\bar{T}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{R;\bar{T}}
, that

\lVert v\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}<R,\qquad\lVert w\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}<R,\qquad\lVert\Psi(\bm{v})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}<R,\qquad\lVert\Psi(\bm{w})\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}<R.

Choosing
\bar{T}
still smaller, if necessary, we can arrange that for some
c<1
,

\lVert\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{v})-\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{w})\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}<c\lVert\bm{v}-\bm{w}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}},

showing that
\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}
is a contraction on
\mathfrak{B}_{R;\bar{T}}
.

By Banach’s fixed-point theorem there exists a unique fixed point for
\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{R;\bar{T}}
. It suffices to argue that a fixed point to
\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}
is also a fixed point to
\bm{\Psi}
. The following is due to
[
Per20
, Thm. 5.15]
. Denote for the sake of notation,
\bm{w}=\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{w})
and
\bm{v}\coloneq\bm{\Psi}(\bm{w})
. We have
\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}(\bm{v})=\bm{\Psi}(\bm{w})=\bm{v}
, hence
\bm{v}
is itself a fixed point to
\bm{\Psi}^{\circ 2}
, yielding by uniqueness that
\bm{v}=\bm{w}
. One can furthermore show that this fixed point is in fact unique in all of
\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}
; it suffices to compare two putative solutions in
\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}
and similar estimates to those above show that they must be equal on a small time interval. Continuity then gives equality in all of
[0,\bar{T}]
.
∎

The utility of Proposition
3.9
is that it allows us to show existence and uniqueness of a suitable notion of solution to (
1.1
) by setting
\rho\coloneq\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+w
. We call this solution paracontrolled and are now in a position to give a rigorous definition (cf. (
1.12
)–(
1.13
) for a formal motivation.)

Definition 3.10
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. For every
S\leq T
, we call
\rho\colon[0,S]\to\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
a paracontrolled solution to (
1.1
) on
[0,S]
with enhancement
\mathbb{X}
and initial data
\rho_{0}
, if

•

the triple
\bm{w}\coloneq(w,w^{\prime},w^{\#})
of distributions

w\coloneq\rho-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\qquad w^{\prime}\coloneq\nabla\Phi_{w}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}},\qquad w^{\#}\coloneq\rho-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}];

satisfies
\bm{w}\in\mathscr{D}_{S}
(see Definition
3.4
);

•

the solution
\rho
satisfies
\rho=P\rho_{0}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\rho\nabla\Phi_{\rho}]+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}
, where the product
\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\rho\nabla\Phi_{\rho}]
needs to be interpreted in the renormalised sense, that is

\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\rho\nabla\Phi_{\rho}]\coloneq\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})]

with
\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})
defined as in Lemma
3.8
.

In particular it follows by Definition
3.4
, that
\lim_{t\to 0}\rho_{t}=\rho_{0}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.
We call
\rho\colon[0,S)\to\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
a paracontrolled solution on
[0,S)
, if
\rho
is a paracontrolled solution on
[0,S^{\prime}]
for every
S^{\prime}<S
.

Using the uniqueness of the fixed point in Proposition
3.9
, one can show that paracontrolled solutions are unique.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
,
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and
S\leq T
. Then there exists at most one paracontrolled solution
\rho\colon[0,S]\to\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
to (
1.1
) on
[0,S]
with enhancement
\mathbb{X}
and initial data
\rho_{0}
.

Proof.

Consider two paracontrolled solutions
\rho
,
\widetilde{\rho}
, which by definition are associated to two triples
\bm{w}
,
\widetilde{\bm{w}}
. However, these triples must both satisfy by definition the same fixed-point equation (
3.6
), which has a unique solution by Proposition
3.9
. Therefore both paracontrolled solutions must agree.
∎

In the following lemma, we prove the existence of a paracontrolled solution given an abstract enhancement.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Let
\bar{T}\in(0,T\wedge 1]
be as in Proposition
3.9
and
\bm{w}=(w,w^{\prime},w^{\#})\in\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}
be the fixed point of (
3.6
) constructed therein. Then
\rho=\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+w\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
is the unique paracontrolled solution to (
1.1
) on
[0,\bar{T}]
with enhancement
\mathbb{X}
and initial data
\rho_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\bm{w}=(w,w^{\prime},w^{\#})\in\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}
be the fixed point to (
3.6
) and
\rho\coloneq\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+w
. It follows by definition,

w=\rho-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}},\qquad w^{\prime}=\nabla\Phi_{w}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}},\qquad w^{\#}=w-\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]=\rho-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}-\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]

and

\rho=P\rho_{0}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\Omega^{\#}(\bm{w})]+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}.

Hence,
\rho
is a paracontrolled solution to (
1.1
) on
[0,\bar{T}]
with enhancement
\mathbb{X}
and initial data
\rho_{0}
, which is unique by Lemma
3.11
.
∎

The next lemma shows that paracontrolled solutions are locally Lipschitz continuous in the noise enhancement and the initial data.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
R>0
,
\mathbb{X}=(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{X}},{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\!}_{\textsf{X}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{X}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{X}}),\mathbb{Y}=(\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{Y}},{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\!}_{\textsf{Y}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{Y}},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{Y}})\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and
\rho_{0}^{\textsf{X}},\rho_{0}^{\textsf{Y}}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
be such that

\max\{\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}},\lVert\mathbb{Y}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}},\lVert\rho_{0}^{\textsf{X}}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}},\lVert\rho_{0}^{\textsf{Y}}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}}\}<R.

Then there exists some
\bar{T}=\bar{T}(R)\in(0,T]
with the following properties

1.

There exist solutions
\bm{w}_{\textsf{X}}\coloneq(w_{\textsf{X}},w^{\prime}_{\textsf{X}},w^{\#}_{\textsf{X}})
and
\bm{w}_{\textsf{Y}}\coloneq(w_{\textsf{Y}},w^{\prime}_{\textsf{Y}},w^{\#}_{\textsf{Y}})
to

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} w_{\textsf{X}}&\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}_{\textsf{X}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{X}}]+w^{\#}_{\textsf{X}},\quad w^{\prime}_{\textsf{X}}\coloneq\nabla\Phi_{w_{\textsf{X}}}+\nabla\Phi_{{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\!}_{\textsf{X}}},\\
w^{\#}_{\textsf{X}}&\coloneq P\rho_{0}^{\textsf{X}}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\Omega^{\#}_{\textsf{X}}(\bm{w}_{\textsf{X}})],\end{aligned}\end{cases}

and

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} w_{\textsf{Y}}&\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}_{\textsf{Y}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{Y}}]+w^{\#}_{\textsf{Y}},\quad w^{\prime}_{\textsf{Y}}\coloneq\nabla\Phi_{w_{\textsf{Y}}}+\nabla\Phi_{{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\!}_{\textsf{Y}}},\\
w^{\#}_{\textsf{Y}}&\coloneq P\rho_{0}^{\textsf{Y}}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\Omega^{\#}_{\textsf{Y}}(\bm{w}_{\textsf{Y}})],\end{aligned}\end{cases}

on
[0,\bar{T}]
by an application of Proposition
3.9
. Here,
\Omega^{\#}_{\textsf{X}}(\bm{w}_{\textsf{X}})
and
\Omega^{\#}_{\textsf{Y}}(\bm{w}_{\textsf{Y}})
are defined as in Lemma
3.8
with noises
\mathbb{X}
,
\mathbb{Y}
respectively.

2.

Setting

\rho_{\textsf{X}}\coloneq\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{X}}+{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\!}_{\textsf{X}}+w_{\textsf{X}},\qquad\rho_{\textsf{Y}}\coloneq\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textsf{Y}}+{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\!}_{\textsf{Y}}+w_{\textsf{Y}},

one has

\begin{split}&\max\{\lVert w_{\textsf{X}}-w_{\textsf{Y}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert w^{\#}_{\textsf{X}}-w^{\#}_{\textsf{Y}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},\lVert w^{\#}_{\textsf{X}}-w^{\#}_{\textsf{Y}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{2\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta^{\#}}},\lVert\rho_{\textsf{X}}-\rho_{\textsf{Y}}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;\bar{T}}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}\}\\
&\lesssim\lVert\rho^{\textsf{X}}_{0}-\rho^{\textsf{Y}}_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}}+\lVert\mathbb{X}-\mathbb{Y}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{\bar{T}}}(\lVert\bm{w}_{\textsf{X}}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}+\lVert\bm{w}_{\textsf{Y}}\rVert_{\mathscr{D}_{\bar{T}}}+\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{\bar{T}}}+\lVert\mathbb{Y}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{\bar{T}}}+1)^{2}.\end{split}

Proof.

The claim follows as in Lemma
3.8
, using the trilinearity of the equation.
∎

3.2
Maximal Time of Existence

To establish a maximal time of existence for paracontrolled solutions to (
1.1
), we iterate the construction of Subsection
3.1
(Theorem
3.15
).

Let
(\alpha,p,q,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\#},\beta_{0},\kappa,\eta)
satisfy (
3.1
). Assume we have constructed a solution
\bm{w}\in\mathscr{D}_{T_{1}}
to (
3.6
) until time
T_{1}\in(0,T)
. Given the initial data
\rho_{T_{1}}=\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}}+w_{T_{1}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and an enhancement
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
, our paracontrolled Ansatz for the continuation is
\widetilde{\bm{w}}=(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}^{\prime},\widetilde{w}^{\#})
, where for
t\geq 0
,

\widetilde{w}_{t}=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}]_{t}+\widetilde{w}^{\#}_{t}

(3.12)

with Gubinelli derivative

\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t}=\nabla\Phi_{\widetilde{w}_{t}}+\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}}

(3.13)

and paracontrolled remainder

\begin{split}\widetilde{w}^{\#}_{t}&=P_{t}(\rho_{T_{1}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}})+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\widetilde{w}\nabla\Phi_{\widetilde{w}}]_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\widetilde{w}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}]_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}\nabla\Phi_{\widetilde{w}}]_{t}\\
&\quad+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}]_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}]_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}]_{t}\\
&\quad+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}]_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}]_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\widetilde{w}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}]_{t}\\
&\quad+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\widetilde{w}]_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\Phi_{\widetilde{w}}]_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathscr{P}_{T_{1}}(\widetilde{\bm{w}},\mathbb{X})]_{t},\end{split}

(3.14)

where the renormalised product
\mathscr{P}_{T_{1}}(\widetilde{\bm{w}},\mathbb{X})_{t}
is given by

\begin{split}\mathscr{P}_{T_{1}}(\widetilde{\bm{w}},\mathbb{X})_{t}&\coloneq\mathscr{C}(\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t},\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}]_{t},\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}})+\mathscr{C}(\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t},\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}]_{t},\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t})\\
&\quad+(\widetilde{w}^{\#}_{t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}]_{t}-\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}]_{t})\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}}\\
&\quad+(\nabla\Phi_{\widetilde{w}_{t}^{\#}}+\nabla\nabla\cdot\Phi_{\mathcal{I}[\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}]_{t}}-\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{{T_{1}}+\,\cdot\,}}]_{t})\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}\\
&\quad+\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}-\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t}(\nabla P_{t}\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]_{T_{1}}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}}+\nabla^{2}P_{t}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}]_{T_{1}}\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}).\end{split}

(3.15)

Hence, the only difference between the first iteration (Proposition
3.9
) and subsequent steps is the initial data
\rho_{T_{1}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}}
in (
3.14
) and the additional term

\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t}(\nabla P_{t}\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]_{T_{1}}\mathbin{\varodot}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}}+\nabla^{2}P_{t}\mathcal{I}[\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}]_{T_{1}}\mathbin{\varodot}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t})

in the renormalised product (
3.15
).

Remark 3.14
.

The paracontrolled triple
\widetilde{\bm{w}}
of the second iteration step is related to the paracontrolled triple
\bm{w}
of the first iteration step by

\widetilde{w}_{t}=w_{T_{1}+t},\qquad\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{t}=w^{\prime}_{T_{1}+t},\qquad\widetilde{w}^{\#}_{t}=w^{\#}_{T_{1}+t}+P_{t}(w_{T_{1}}-w^{\#}_{T_{1}})

(for those
t
such that both
\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}
and
\bm{w}_{T_{1}+t}
exist.) The additional terms in
\widetilde{w}^{\#}
and
\mathscr{P}_{T_{1}}(\widetilde{\bm{w}},\mathbb{X})
ensure that the initial data in (
3.14
) can be expressed in terms of
\rho_{T_{1}}
,
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}}
and
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}}
. Since we assumed
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
, this allows us to restart the equation as long as
\rho_{T_{1}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. The price to pay is that the dynamics of the paracontrolled remainder changes at
T_{1}
, hence the triple will no longer be continuous on the full time interval of existence. However, this will not be an issue, as we are primarily interested in the dynamics of the solution

\widetilde{\rho}_{t}\coloneq\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+\,\cdot\,}]_{t}+\widetilde{w}^{\#}_{t}=\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}+\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{T_{1}+t}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[w^{\prime}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}]_{T_{1}+t}+w^{\#}_{T_{1}+t}=\rho_{T_{1}+t}

which will still be continuous on the full time interval.

We can now use a technique inspired by
[
MW17a
, Derivation of (1.27)]
to establish a maximal time of existence
T_{\textnormal{max}}\leq T
and a paracontrolled solution on
[0,T_{\textnormal{max}})
.

Theorem 3.15
.

Let
(\alpha,p,q,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\#},\beta_{0},\kappa,\eta)
satisfy (
3.1
),
T>0
,
\mathbb{X}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Then there exists a
T_{\textnormal{max}}\in(0,T]
and a unique paracontrolled solution
\rho\colon[0,T_{\textnormal{max}})\to\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
to (
1.1
) on
[0,T_{\textnormal{max}})
with enhancement
\mathbb{X}
and initial data
\rho_{0}
, such that

T_{\textnormal{max}}=T\qquad\text{or}\qquad\lim_{t\uparrow T_{\textnormal{max}}}\lVert\rho_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}=\infty.

Proof.

Let
(\alpha,p,q,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\#},\beta_{0},\kappa,\eta)
satisfy (
3.1
). Since
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we can apply Proposition
3.9
and run the equation for some small time
\bar{t}_{0}=\bar{t}_{0}(\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}},\lVert\rho_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}})\leq T
. By construction, we obtain
\rho_{t_{0}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
for each
0<t_{0}\leq\bar{t}_{0}
.

For the second iteration step, we choose a new tuple of exponents
(\alpha,\infty,\infty,\beta_{1},\beta^{\prime}_{1},\beta^{\#}_{1},\alpha+1,\kappa,\eta_{1})
satisfying the assumptions (
3.1
) and

\eta_{1}\in\Bigl[\Bigl(\frac{\beta_{1}-(\alpha+1)}{2}\Bigr)\vee\Bigl(\frac{\beta^{\#}_{1}-(\alpha+1)}{4}\Bigr),\frac{\alpha+3}{2}\Bigr)\cap\Bigl(\frac{-2\alpha-4}{2},\frac{\alpha+3}{2}\Bigr).

(3.16)

Using the assumption (
3.16
), we can control the additional term appearing in (
3.15
) via Bony’s estimates (Lemma
A.4
) and Schauder’s estimates (Lemma
A.6
).

Let
R>0
be such that
\lVert\mathbb{X}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}\leq R
and assume that
0<t_{0}<\bar{t}_{0}
satisfies
\lVert\rho_{t_{0}}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}\leq R
. We can then use the decomposition (
3.12
)–(
3.15
) and an argument analogous to Proposition
3.9
to re-start the equation from
\rho_{t_{0}}
and run it until time
t_{0}+T(R)
for some
T(R)>0
.

Next, assume
\lVert\rho_{t_{0}+T(R)/2}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}\leq R
, we can then re-start the equation at time
t_{0}+T(R)/2
from
\rho_{t_{0}+T(R)/2}
and run it until
t_{0}+T(R)/2+T(R)
with the same exponents. We can then continue this procedure on the intervals

[0,\bar{t}_{0}],\quad[t_{0},t_{0}+T(R)],\quad\Bigl[t_{0}+\frac{T(R)}{2},t_{0}+\frac{T(R)}{2}+T(R)\Bigr],\quad\Bigl[t_{0}+T(R),t_{0}+2T(R)\Bigr],\quad\ldots

(3.17)

until the first time
t\in[t_{0},T]
such that
\lVert\rho_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}>R
, or, if such a
t
does not exist, until
T
. We denote this time horizon by
T_{\textnormal{max}}^{(R)}
. This yields a paracontrolled solution
\rho\colon[0,T_{\textnormal{max}}^{(R)}]\to\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
to (
1.1
) with enhancement
\mathbb{X}
and initial data
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, where we used that the intervals (
3.17
) had some overlap as in
[
MW17a
, Derivation of (1.27)]
to ensure that
\rho
is independent of the choice of iteration used in its construction. Furthermore, the paracontrolled solution is unique by an application of Lemma
3.11
.
The sequence
(T^{(R)}_{\max})_{R>0}
is increasing, hence we may define
T_{\textnormal{max}}\coloneq\sup_{R>0}T_{\textnormal{max}}^{(R)}=\lim_{R\to\infty}T_{\textnormal{max}}^{(R)}\in(0,T]
. Assume
T_{\textnormal{max}}<T
, then

\lim_{t\uparrow T_{\textnormal{max}}}\lVert\rho_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}=\lim_{R\uparrow\infty}\lVert\rho_{T_{\textnormal{max}}^{(R)}}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}>\lim_{R\uparrow\infty}R=\infty.

This yields the claim.
∎

The principal issue in comparing paracontrolled solutions is that the maximal time of existence may depend on the noise enhancement. Hence, one needs to find a metric space of functions that can blow up at separate, finite times. A suitable space was introduced in
[
Cha+22
, Sec. 1.5.1]
, whose construction we follow:

Definition 3.16
.

Let
(F,\lVert\,\cdot\,\rVert_{F})
be a normed vector space and let

of continuous functions on

We equip
F^{\textnormal{sol}}_{T}
with a suitable metric.

Lemma 3.18
.

There exists a metric
D_{T}^{F}
on
F^{\textnormal{sol}}_{T}
such that
D_{T}^{F}(f_{n},f)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
if and only if for every
L>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{L}]}\lVert f_{n}(t)-f(t)\rVert_{F}=0,

where

T_{L}\coloneq T\wedge L\wedge\inf\{t\in[0,T]:\lVert f_{n}(t)\rVert_{F}>L\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \lVert f(t)\rVert_{F}>L\}.

Proof.

The existence of such a metric follows by
[
Cha+22
, Lem. 1.2]
.
∎

Finally, we equip
F^{\textnormal{sol}}_{T}
with a weight at
0
to allow for irregular initial data.

.

and equip
F^{\textnormal{sol}}_{\eta;T}
with the metric

given by

D_{\eta;T}^{F}(u,v)\coloneq D_{T}^{F}(u_{\eta\textnormal{-}\textnormal{wt}},v_{\eta\textnormal{-}\textnormal{wt}}),\qquad u,v\in F^{\textnormal{sol}}_{\eta;T}.

3.15
are elements of
(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{\textnormal{sol}}_{\eta;T}
.

.

.

Proof.

The paracontrolled solution constructed in Theorem
3.15
is continuous and exists until it blows up in
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, from which the claim follows.
∎

3.3
The Renormalised Solution

In this subsection we combine the deterministic solution theory (Lemma
3.20
) with the stochastic existence of the renormalised enhancement (Theorem
2.3
) to construct the renormalised solution to (
1.1
) as a random variable (Theorem
3.22
, Part
1
). We then show that the renormalised solution is the limit in probability of solutions to (
1.14
) (Theorem
3.22
, Part
2
).

.
