) with enhancement
\mathbb{X}
(resp.
(\mathbb{X}^{\delta})_{\delta>0}
) and initial data
\rho_{0}
in the sense of Definition
3.10
(for every
\delta>0
). The random variables
(\rho^{\delta})_{\delta>0}
coincide with the mild solutions (
1.14
).

2.

It holds that
\rho^{\delta}\to\rho
in
(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{\textnormal{sol}}_{\eta;T}
in probability as
\delta\to 0
. In particular for each
\lambda>0
one has that

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sum_{L=1}^{\infty}\frac{2^{-L}}{L}\frac{\lVert\rho-\rho^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T_{L}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}}{1+\lVert\rho-\rho^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{\eta;T_{L}}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}}>\lambda\Bigr)=0,

(3.18)

where for every
L\in\mathbb{N}
we denote

T_{L}\coloneq T\wedge L\wedge\inf\Bigl\{t\in[0,T]:\lVert\rho_{\eta\textnormal{-}\textnormal{wt}}(t)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}>L\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \lVert\rho_{\eta\textnormal{-}\textnormal{wt}}^{\delta}(t)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}>L\Bigr\}.

Proof.

Let
(\alpha,p,q,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\#},\beta_{0},\kappa,\eta)
satisfy (
3.1
). The enhancements
\mathbb{X}
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\delta})_{\delta>0}
exist almost surely by an application of Theorem
2.3
. It then follows by Lemma
3.20
that
\rho
and
(\rho^{\delta})_{\delta>0}
are random variables in
(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{\textnormal{sol}}_{\eta;T}
. By Lemma
3.18
and the local Lipschitz continuity of the solution map in the enhancement (Lemma
3.13
) we deduce that for every
\lambda>0
there exists some
\nu>0
such that
\lVert\mathbb{X}-\mathbb{X}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}\leq\nu
implies
D_{\eta;T}^{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}(\rho,\rho^{\delta})\leq\lambda
.
Hence, by an application of Theorem
2.3
,

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(D_{\eta;T}^{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}(\rho,\rho^{\delta})>\lambda\Bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}(\lVert\mathbb{X}-\mathbb{X}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}>\nu)\to 0\quad\text{as}\penalty\ \delta\to 0,

which yields that
\rho^{\delta}\to\rho
in
(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))_{\eta;T}^{\textnormal{sol}}
in probability. The convergence (
3.18
) then follows by the explicit form of
D_{\eta;T}^{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+1}}
, see
[
Cha+22
, Sec. 1.5.1]
.
∎

Example 3.23
.

The same arguments as in the proof of Theorem
3.22
, Part
1
, also allow us to construct the mild solution to (
1.3
) as a paracontrolled solution to (
1.1
) with enhancement
\mathbb{X}^{(\varepsilon)}_{\delta}=(\varepsilon^{1/2}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta},\varepsilon\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textnormal{can}}^{\delta},\varepsilon^{3/2}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}_{\textnormal{can}}^{\delta},\varepsilon\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta})
(and heterogeneity
\sigma=\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}
). To obtain a limit for vanishing correlation lengths
\delta
, one then needs to assume that the noise intensity
\varepsilon
decays sufficiently quickly depending on the behaviour of the renormalisation. This will be the focus of an upcoming work
[
MM24
]
.

Appendix A
Besov and Hölder–Besov Spaces

Throughout the following section, all properties are given for mappings or distributions on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
d,n\in\mathbb{N}
.

A.1
Besov Spaces

Applying essentially the same arguments as in the proof of
[
BCD11
, Prop. 2.10]
there exists a
dyadic partition of unity
, i.e. a pair of non-negative, radially symmetric and compactly supported smooth functions
\varrho_{-1},\varrho_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};[0,1])
such that
\text{supp}(\varrho_{-1})\subset B(0,1/2)
,
\text{supp}(\varrho_{0})\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:9/32\leq\lvert x\rvert\leq 1\}
and
\sum_{k=-1}^{\infty}\varrho_{k}(x)=1
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where we denote
\varrho_{k}(x)\coloneq\varrho_{0}(2^{-k}x)
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

For every
k\geq-1
we define the Littlewood–Paley block
\Delta_{k}
to be the Fourier multiplier
\Delta_{k}u\coloneq\mathscr{F}^{-1}(\varrho_{k}\mathscr{F}u)
and set

\Delta_{<k}u\coloneq\sum_{l=-1}^{k-1}\Delta_{l}u.

As with the Fourier transform, we initially define these operators on smooth functions and then extend them by duality to
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Definition A.1

(Besov spaces)
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
p,q\in[1,\infty]
. We define the non-homogeneous Besov
space
\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
to be the completion of the smooth functions
C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
under the norm

\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\coloneq\lVert(2^{k\alpha}\lVert\Delta_{k}u\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}}\rVert_{\ell^{q}},

which is extended to vector resp. matrix-valued functions in a natural componentwise manner. Here
\ell^{q}
denotes the usual space of
q
-summable sequences (or bounded when
q=\infty
.) When
p=q=\infty
we recall the shorthand
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\coloneq\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and call
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
the Hölder–Besov space.

Remark A.2
.

Note that the dyadic partition of unity obtained by
[
BCD11
, Prop. 2.10]
is built from
\widetilde{\varrho}_{-1}
,
\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}
with
\text{supp}(\widetilde{\varrho}_{-1})\subset B(0,4/3)
and
\text{supp}(\widetilde{\varrho}_{0})\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:3/4\leq\lvert x\rvert\leq 8/3\}
. However, for our purposes it is convenient to rescale these functions by a factor of
3/8
so that the only integer in the support of
\varrho_{-1}
is
0
. Since the Besov spaces are independent of the chosen dyadic partition of unity
[
BCD11
, Cor. 2.70]
this change is harmless.

Besov spaces enjoy a number of useful properties which we list below. Proofs of the following statements can be found in
[
BCD11
,
GIP15
]
.

1.

Embeddings: There exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
,
1\leq p_{1}\leq p_{2}\leq\infty
and
1\leq q_{1}\leq q_{2}\leq\infty
,

\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha-d(1/p_{1}-1/p_{2})}_{p_{2},q_{2}}}\leq C\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}.

(A.1)

We also have the following continuous embeddings,

\displaystyle\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{p,q}},\quad\alpha<\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{p,q^{\prime}}},\quad\alpha<\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R},\penalty\ q<q^{\prime}\in[1,\infty].

2.

Relations to
L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
-spaces: For all
p\in[1,\infty]
, one has,

\displaystyle\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{p,\infty}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{L^{p}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{p,1}}.

We regularly work in a scale of interpolation spaces which relate temporal and spatial regularity and are suitable for solutions to parabolic PDEs.

Definition A.3

(Interpolation spaces)
.

Let
T>0
,
\eta\geq 0
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
\kappa\in(0,1]
. We define the norm

\lVert u\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\coloneq\max\{\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha-2\kappa}},\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\},

and the spaces

\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})=C_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha-2\kappa}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\cap C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

We set

\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\coloneq\mathscr{L}_{0;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\coloneq C_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha-2\kappa}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\cap C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

and by an abuse of notation understand
\mathscr{L}^{0}_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})=C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
.

A.2
Paraproducts

For
u,v\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
we define the paraproduct
\mathbin{\varolessthan}
and resonant product
\mathbin{\varodot}
by

u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v\coloneq\sum_{k\geq-1}\Delta_{<k-1}u\Delta_{k}v,\qquad u\mathbin{\varodot}v\coloneq\sum_{\lvert k-l\rvert\leq 1}\Delta_{l}u\Delta_{k}v.

Formally one has the decomposition
uv=u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v+v\mathbin{\varolessthan}u+u\mathbin{\varodot}v
. Conditions under which this decomposition is valid for (time-dependent) distributions
u
and
v
are given by the following version of Bony’s estimates. These operators naturally extend to vector-valued and matrix-valued objects as either inner or outer products. Where the precise meaning is not clear from context it will be specified in the text.

Lemma A.4

(Bony’s estimates)
.

Let
T>0
and
\eta,\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\geq 0
be such that
\eta=\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}
.

•

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
u\in C_{\eta_{1};T}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and
v\in C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, then
u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and

\lVert u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim_{\beta}\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta_{1};T}L^{\infty}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

•

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
\alpha<0
,
u\in C_{\eta_{1};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and
v\in C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, then
u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and

\lVert u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta_{1};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

•

Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
with
\alpha+\beta>0
,
u\in C_{\eta_{1};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and
v\in C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, then
u\mathbin{\varodot}v\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and

\lVert u\mathbin{\varodot}v\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta_{1};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

Proof.

The result is a direct consequence of
[
GIP15
, Lem. 2.1]
.
∎

A.3
Parabolic and Elliptic Regularity Estimates

We will make use of the following interpolation inequality. Let
x\geq 0
and
\gamma\in[0,1]
, then

0\leq 1-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-x}\leq x^{\gamma}.

(A.2)

We also apply the following rapid-decay inequality. For any
r>0
, uniformly in
x\geq 0
,

x^{r}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-x}\lesssim 1.

(A.3)

The operators
P
,
\mathcal{I}
and
\Phi
introduced in Subsection
1.1
and their accompanying kernels
\mathscr{H}
and
\mathscr{G}
can be generalized to
\mathbb{T}^{d}

mutatis mutandis
. For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we also apply
\mathcal{I}
to
f=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})\colon[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
by setting
\mathcal{I}[f]=(\mathcal{I}[f_{1}],\ldots,\mathcal{I}[f_{n}])
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\alpha\leq\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
p,q,p^{\prime},q^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]
be such that
p\geq p^{\prime}
and
q\geq q^{\prime}
. Then for any
t>0
,

\lVert P_{t}f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,q}}\lesssim\bigl(1\vee t^{-\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}}\bigr)\bigl(1\vee t^{-\frac{d}{2}(\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{p})}\bigr)\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}^{\alpha}}.

(A.4)

Secondly, if
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\alpha+2
then for any
t>0
,

\lVert(P_{t}-1)f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}}\lesssim t^{\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,q}}.

(A.5)

Proof.

We first show (
A.4
), which is an easy consequence of the Besov embedding and the regularising effect of the heat flow. By (
A.1
) for any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
p,q,p^{\prime},q^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]
with
p\geq p^{\prime}
and
q\geq q^{\prime}
, it holds that,

\lVert P_{t}f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,q}}\lesssim\lVert P_{t}f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta+d(1/p^{\prime}-1/p)}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}}.

We now apply the semigroup property and the regularizing effect of the heat flow (
[
BCD11
, Lem. 2.4]
& (
A.3
)),

\lVert P_{t}f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta+d(1/p^{\prime}-1/p)}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}}\lesssim\bigl(1\vee t^{-\frac{d}{2}(\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{p})}\bigr)\lVert P_{t/2}f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}}\lesssim\bigl(1\vee t^{-\frac{d}{2}(\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{p})}\bigr)\bigl(1\vee t^{-\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}}\bigr)\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}},

which yields (
A.4
).
The bound (
A.5
) can be found in
[
MW17a
, Prop. A.13]
, who cite
[
MW17
, Prop. 6]
for a proof in the full space. We provide a short argument. We consider the Littlewood–Paley blocks
\Delta_{k}(P_{t}-1)u=(P_{t}-1)\Delta_{k}u
for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}
. Since
(P_{t}-1)\Delta_{-1}u=0
, we may assume
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We apply
[
BCD11
, Lem. 2.4 & Lem. 2.1]
to obtain the existence of some
c>0
such that

\begin{split}\lVert(P_{t}-1)\Delta_{k}f\rVert_{L^{p}}&\leq\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\partial_{s}P_{s}\Delta_{k}f\rVert_{L^{p}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus=\int_{0}^{t}\lVert P_{s}\Delta\Delta_{k}f\rVert_{L^{p}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\\
&\lesssim\lVert\Delta\Delta_{k}f\rVert_{L^{p}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-cs2^{2k}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\lesssim\lVert\Delta_{k}f\rVert_{L^{p}}2^{2k}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-cs2^{2k}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\\
&\lesssim\lVert\Delta_{k}f\rVert_{L^{p}}(1-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-ct2^{2k}})\lesssim\lVert\Delta_{k}f\rVert_{L^{p}}t^{\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}}2^{k(\beta-\alpha)}.\end{split}

In the last inequality, we applied (
A.2
) with
(\beta-\alpha)/2\in[0,1]
. This yields the claim using the definition of the
\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
-norm.
∎

We can now establish Schauder estimates similar to
[
GIP15
, Lem. A.9]
and
[
CC18
, Prop. 2.7]
.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
T>0
,
\eta\geq 0
,
\alpha\leq\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
\kappa\in[0,1]
and
p,q\in[1,\infty]
. Then the following hold

1.

If
\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}+\frac{d}{2p}\leq\eta
, then

\lVert Pf\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim(1\vee T^{-\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}})(1\vee T^{-\frac{d}{2p}})(1\wedge T)^{\eta}\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\alpha}}

(A.6)

and
P\colon\mathcal{B}_{p,q}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is a continuous map.

2.

If
\eta^{\prime}\in[0,1)
,
\eta\leq\eta^{\prime}
and
\beta<\alpha+2
are such that
\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}\vee\kappa\leq 1-(\eta^{\prime}-\eta)
, then

\lVert\mathcal{I}[f]\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert f\rVert_{C_{\eta^{\prime};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}

(A.7)

and
\mathcal{I}\colon C_{\eta^{\prime};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is a continuous map.
In particular if
T\leq 1
, then

\lVert\mathcal{I}[f]\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta;T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim(T^{1-\frac{\beta-\alpha}{2}-(\eta^{\prime}-\eta)}\vee T^{1-\kappa-(\eta^{\prime}-\eta)})\lVert f\rVert_{C_{\eta^{\prime};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}.

3.

Furthermore,
\mathcal{I}\colon C_{T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is a continuous map.

Proof.

The proofs of (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) are simple consequences of Lemma
A.5
, the semigroup property and the definition of the interpolation spaces, Definition
A.3
. The continuity of
t\mapsto(1\wedge t)^{\eta}P_{t}u_{0}
and
t\mapsto(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\mathcal{I}[f]_{t}
follows from the fact that the same statement is true for smooth
u_{0}
,
f
, and then by taking limits along a smooth approximating sequence.
∎

Assume
\theta\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
is smooth and such that
\partial^{\nu}\theta
is of at most polynomial growth for each multi-indix
\nu\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
.
Additionally assume that
\theta
satisfies the
reality condition

\overline{\theta(\omega)}=\theta(-\omega),\qquad\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}.

(A.8)

We define the Fourier multiplier acting on
u\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
by the expression

\theta(D)u\coloneq\mathscr{F}^{-1}(\theta\widehat{u}).

The polynomial growth condition on all partial derivatives and (
A.8
) ensure that
\theta(D)
maps real-valued distributions to real-valued distributions, a result which we generalize in the following lemma.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
,
p,q\in[1,\infty]
,
u\in\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
k=2\lfloor 1+d/2\rfloor
. Assume that
\theta\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
satisfies
\theta\in C^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\};\mathbb{C})
,
\theta(0)=0
, the reality condition (
A.8
) and that there exist some
m\in\mathbb{R}
,
C>0
, such that for any multi-index
\nu\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
with
\lvert\nu\rvert\leq k
,

\lvert\partial^{\nu}\theta(x)\rvert\leq C\lvert x\rvert^{m-\lvert\nu\rvert},\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}.

Then,

\lVert\theta(D)u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha-m}_{p,q}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}}.

Proof.

Since
u
is periodic the only frequency contained in the support of
\varrho_{-1}
is
\omega=0
, hence
\theta(D)\Delta_{-1}u=0
. The remaining Littlewood–Paley blocks can then be addressed directly with
[
BCD11
, Lem. 2.2]
.
∎

Lemma
A.7
leads directly to a control on solutions to Poisson’s equation and their derivatives.

Lemma A.8
.

For any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, it follows that

\lVert\mathscr{G}\ast u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha-2}_{p,q}},\qquad\lVert\nabla\mathscr{G}\ast u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,q}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha-1}_{p,q}}.

Proof.

Simply apply Lemma
A.7
to the multipliers
\theta_{1}(\omega)=\frac{1}{\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\omega\neq 0}
and
\theta_{2}(\omega)=\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\omega}{\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\omega\neq 0}
.
∎

A.4
Commutator Results

Many of the results presented below are analogues and simple extensions of similar results found in
[
Per13
,
CC18
]
to time-weighted spaces,
C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma A.9
.

Let
T>0
,
\eta,\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\geq 0
be such that
\eta=\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}
,
\alpha\in(-\infty,1)
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
k=2\lfloor 1+d/2\rfloor
. Assume
\theta\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
satisfies
\theta\in C^{k+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\};\mathbb{C})
,
\theta(0)=0
, (
A.8
) and that there exist some
m\in\mathbb{R}
,
C>0
, such that for any multi-index
\nu\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
with
\lvert\nu\rvert\leq k+1
,

\lvert\partial^{\nu}\theta(x)\rvert\leq C\lvert x\rvert^{m-\lvert\nu\rvert},\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}.

Let
u\in C_{\eta_{1};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and
v\in C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, then
\theta(D)(u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v)-u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\theta(D)v\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta-m}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and

\lVert\theta(D)(u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v)-u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\theta(D)v\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta-m}}\lesssim\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta_{1};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

Proof.

The result is a simple extension of
[
Per13
, Lem. 5.3.20]
and
[
CC18
, Lem. A.1]
to functions with prescribed blow-up in
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
at
t=0
.
∎

Next, we consider the commutator between the operators
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathbin{\varolessthan}
, a result reminiscent of
[
CC18
, Prop. 2.7]
.

Lemma A.10
.

Let
T>0
,
\eta,\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in[0,1)
be such that
\eta=\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}
,
\kappa>0
,
\alpha\in(-\infty,(1\wedge 2\kappa))
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
m\in(0,2)
. For every
u\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta_{1};T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and
v\in C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, it holds that
\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]-u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and

\lVert\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]-u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert u\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta_{1};T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

(A.9)

Proof.

Let
u\in\mathscr{L}_{\eta_{1};T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and
v\in C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, we first prove the bound (
A.9
) and then the regularity
\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]-u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
. Let
t\in[0,T]
, by definition,

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]_{t}-u_{t}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]_{t}&=\int_{0}^{t}P_{t-s}(u(s)\mathbin{\varolessthan}v(s))-u(t)\mathbin{\varolessthan}P_{t-s}v(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\\
&=\int_{0}^{t}P_{t-s}(u(s)\mathbin{\varolessthan}v(s))-u(s)\mathbin{\varolessthan}P_{t-s}v(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus+\int_{0}^{t}(u(s)-u(t))\mathbin{\varolessthan}P_{t-s}v(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus.\end{split}

To bound the first summand we apply
[
Per13
, Lem. 5.3.20]
and use that
\alpha<1
to estimate

\lVert P_{t-s}(u(s)\mathbin{\varolessthan}v(s))-u(s)\mathbin{\varolessthan}P_{t-s}v(s)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}}\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{-m/2}(1\wedge s)^{-\eta}\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta_{1};s}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};s}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

Taking the supremum, we obtain

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}P_{t-s}(u(s)\mathbin{\varolessthan}v(s))-u(s)\mathbin{\varolessthan}P_{t-s}v(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}}\\
&\lesssim\Bigl(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert t-s\rvert^{-m/2}(1\wedge s)^{-\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\Bigr)\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta_{1};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.\end{split}

We can use a case distinction over
t\in[0,1]
and
t\in(1,\infty)
; and the assumptions
\eta<1
,
m<2
, to bound

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert t-s\rvert^{-m/2}(1\wedge s)^{-\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\lesssim_{T}1.

To bound the second summand, we apply Lemma
A.5
and use that
\alpha-2\kappa<0
to estimate

\displaystyle\lVert(u(s)-u(t))\mathbin{\varolessthan}P_{t-s}v(s)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert u(s)-u(t)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha-2\kappa}}\lVert P_{t-s}v(s)\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta+2\kappa+m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa}(1\vee\lvert t-s\rvert^{-\kappa-m/2})(1\wedge s)^{-\eta}\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta_{1};t}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha-2\kappa}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};s}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

Taking the supremum, we obtain

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}(u(s)-u(t))\mathbin{\varolessthan}P_{t-s}v(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}}\\
&\lesssim\Bigl(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa}(1\vee\lvert t-s\rvert^{-\kappa-m/2})(1\wedge s)^{-\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\Bigr)\lVert u\rVert_{C_{\eta_{1};T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha-2\kappa}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.\end{split}

We can use a case distinction over
t\in[0,1]
,
t\in(1,2]
, and
t\in(2,\infty)
; and the assumptions
\eta<1
,
m<2
, to bound

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa}(1\vee\lvert t-s\rvert^{-\kappa-m/2})(1\wedge s)^{-\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mus\lesssim_{T}1.

It follows that

\lVert\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]-u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}}\lesssim_{T}\lVert u\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{\eta_{1};T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lVert v\rVert_{C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}},

which yields (
A.9
).

To establish
\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]-u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, we need to show that
(0,T]\ni t\mapsto(1\wedge t)^{\eta}(\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]_{t}-u_{t}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]_{t})
is continuous and that
\lim_{t\to 0}(1\wedge t)^{\eta}(\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]_{t}-u_{t}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]_{t})
exists in
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
. The continuity in
(0,T]
follows by the completeness of the continuous functions under the supremum norm and by taking limits along a smooth approximating sequence of
v
.
To show the continuity at
0
, we can use the same bounds as in the derivation of (
A.9
) to control for every
t\in(0,1\wedge T]
,

(1\wedge t)^{\eta}\lVert\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]_{t}-u_{t}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}}\lesssim t^{1-m/2},

which implies
\lim_{t\to 0}(1\wedge t)^{\eta}(\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]_{t}-u_{t}\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]_{t})=0\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
. Therefore,
\mathcal{I}[u\mathbin{\varolessthan}v]-u\mathbin{\varolessthan}\mathcal{I}[v]\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+m}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, which yields the claim.
∎

Finally, we present a commutator result between the operators
\mathbin{\varolessthan}
and
\mathbin{\varodot}
.

Lemma A.11
.

Let
T>0
,
\eta,\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{3}\geq 0
be such that
\eta=\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}+\eta_{3}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\beta+\gamma<0
and
\alpha+\beta+\gamma>0
. We define

\mathscr{C}(f,g,h)=(f\mathbin{\varolessthan}g)\mathbin{\varodot}h-f(g\mathbin{\varodot}h),\qquad(f,g,h)\in C_{\eta_{1};T}C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})\times C_{\eta_{2};T}C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})\times C_{\eta_{3};T}C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R}).

Then
\mathscr{C}
extends to a bounded, trilinear operator

\mathscr{C}\colon C_{\eta_{1};T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})\times C_{\eta_{2};T}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})\times C_{\eta_{3};T}\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})\to C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha+\beta+\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})

Proof.

The result is a direct consequence of
[
GIP15
, Lem. 2.4]
.
∎

Appendix B
Shape Coefficient Estimates

In this appendix we bound our shape coefficients in terms of
\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}
and
\lvert\omega_{k}\rvert^{\beta}
for some
\gamma\in[0,1]
and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
. We first consider the shape coefficient for
(cf. Definition
2.18
).

Lemma B.1
.

Let
s,t\geq 0
,
\gamma\in[0,1]
and
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
be such that
\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\not=0
.

1.

In the case
(\omega_{1}\perp\omega_{2})
, we obtain

\mathsf{D}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-4+2\gamma}.

2.

In the case
\lnot(\omega_{1}\perp\omega_{2})
, we obtain

\mathsf{D}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-4+2\gamma}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}+\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-4}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2+2\gamma}.

The implied constants are uniform in
\omega_{1}
,
\omega_{2}
.

Proof.

To bound our shape coefficients, we first derive explicit expressions, a step that was inspired by
[
TW18
, Proof of Thm. 2.1]
. To do so, we evaluate the exponential integrals in (
2.17
) and compute
\mathsf{D}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
through (
2.18
). We can then further decompose

\mathsf{D}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=\mathsf{DL}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})+\mathsf{DE}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2}),

into the
leading term

\mathsf{DL}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
and the
error term

\mathsf{DE}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
. The error term is generated by the zero initial condition of the noise, i.e. the remaining restriction
u_{3},u_{3}^{\prime}\geq 0
in (
2.17
).

Assume
(\omega_{1}\perp\omega_{2})
, then

\mathsf{DL}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=\frac{1}{2^{2}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-4}\Bigl(1-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\lvert t-s\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\lvert t-s\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\lvert t-s\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\Bigr)

and

\begin{split}&\mathsf{DE}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=\frac{1}{2^{3}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-4}\Bigl(2t\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2t\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\\
&\hskip 200.0003pt\ignorespaces+2s\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2s\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\\
&\hskip 270.00041pt\ignorespaces-(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-t\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-s\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})^{2}\Bigr).\end{split}

We first consider
\mathsf{DL}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
and estimate by (
A.2
) for every
\gamma\in[0,1]
,

\mathsf{DL}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-4+2\gamma}.

Next we estimate the error term
\mathsf{DE}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
. By symmetry it is enough to consider
s\leq t
, for which

\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2s\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\leq 0.

We can then proceed to bound the remaining non-negative term of
\mathsf{DE}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
by (
A.2
) and (
A.3
), giving the result in the case
(\omega_{1}\perp\omega_{2})
.

Next assume
\lnot(\omega_{1}\perp\omega_{2})
. We obtain the expressions

\begin{split}&\mathsf{DL}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{2}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\lvert t-s\rvert(\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\lvert t-s\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\\
&\hskip 130.0002pt\ignorespaces+\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}(2-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\lvert t-s\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\lvert t-s\rvert(\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})})\Bigr)\end{split}

(B.1)

and

\begin{split}&\mathsf{DE}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{2}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\\
&\hskip 80.00012pt\ignorespaces\times(2(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{t(\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}-1)(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2t\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\\
&\hskip 100.00015pt\ignorespaces+2(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{s(\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}-1)(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2s\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}))\\
&\hskip 90.00014pt\ignorespaces-\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-t\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-s\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})^{2}\Bigr).\end{split}

(B.2)

The first term in (
B.1
),

\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\lvert t-s\rvert(\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\lvert t-s\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}),

is non-positive so that by (
A.2
),

\mathsf{DL}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-4+2\gamma}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}.

In (
B.2
), we bound the second, non-positive term by
0
. In the first term, we distinguish cases to fix the sign of the prefactor
(\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}
. Assume first
\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}<\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}
and by symmetry
s\leq t
.
We obtain by (
A.2
) and (
A.3
),

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\Bigl((\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{t(\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}-1)(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2t\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\\
&\qquad\quad+(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{s(\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}-1)(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2s\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\Bigr)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)(\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}(1-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-t(\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})})(1-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t-s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\\
&\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\frac{\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2\gamma}}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{t}{t+s}\\
&\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2+2\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}.\end{split}

(B.3)

If instead
\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}<\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}
,
s\leq t
, then

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\\
&\quad\times\Bigl((\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{t(\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}-1)(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2t\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\\
&\qquad\quad+(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{s(\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})}-1)(\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2s\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\Bigr)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\\
&\quad\times(1-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-t(\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}-\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2})})\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t+s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}(1-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-(t-s)\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}})\\
&\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\frac{\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2\gamma}}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{2}}\frac{t}{t+s}\\
&\leq\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2+2\gamma}.\end{split}

(B.4)

By combining (
B.2
) with (
B.3
) and (
B.4
), we arrive at

\mathsf{DE}_{s,t}\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}(\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2+2\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}+\lvert\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2+2\gamma}).

This yields the claim.
∎

Next we bound the shape coefficient
\mathsf{A}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}
, which appears in

,

,

and

(cf. Definition
2.24
).

Lemma B.2
.

Let
s,t\geq 0
,
k=1,2
,
\gamma\in[0,1]
and
C\geq 1
. Then uniformly in
\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
such that
C^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert\leq\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert\leq C\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert
, it holds that

\mathsf{A}_{s,t}^{k}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3})\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert^{2\gamma}\lvert\omega_{3}\rvert^{-1}.

Proof.

The claim follows by the triangle inequality and repeated applications of (
A.2
).
∎

The following lemma controls the shape coefficient
\mathsf{A}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}
, which appears in

,

and

,

(cf. Definition
2.22
).

Lemma B.3
.

Let
s,t\geq 0
,
k,k^{\prime}=1,2
,
\gamma\in[0,1]
and
C,C^{\prime}\geq 1
. Then uniformly in
\omega_{1},\omega_{1}^{\prime},\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
such that
C^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert\leq\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert\leq C\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert
and
(C^{\prime})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}^{\prime}\rvert\leq\lvert\omega_{2}\rvert\leq C^{\prime}\lvert\omega_{1}^{\prime}\rvert
, it holds that

\mathsf{A}^{k,k^{\prime}}_{s,t}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}(\omega_{1},\omega_{1}^{\prime},\omega_{2})\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-1+\gamma}\lvert\omega_{1}^{\prime}\rvert^{-1+\gamma}.

Proof.

The claim follows by the triangle inequality and repeated applications of (
A.2
).
∎

Appendix C
Summation Estimates

C.1
Basic Estimates

We prove a number of summation and discrete convolution estimates that are central to our bounds.

Recall that the Fourier multiplier of the elliptic operator
\partial_{j}\Phi
, denoted by
G^{j}(\omega)=2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\omega^{j}\lvert 2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\omega\rvert^{-2}\mathds{1}_{\omega\neq 0}
for all
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
j=1,2
, frequently appears in the diagrams considered in Subsection
2.2
. The following lemma shows that
\lvert G^{j}(\omega+\omega_{1})-G^{j}(\omega_{1})\rvert
decays like
\lvert\omega+\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}
for every
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}
, which is one order better than
\lvert G^{j}(\omega+\omega_{1})\rvert\lesssim\lvert\omega+\omega_{1}\rvert^{-1}
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
j=1,2
, then uniformly in
\omega,\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
\omega_{1},\omega+\omega_{1}\neq 0
, it holds that

\lvert G^{j}(\omega+\omega_{1})-G^{j}(\omega_{1})\rvert\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega+\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-1}).

Proof.

We compute

G^{j}(\omega+\omega_{1})-G^{j}(\omega_{1})=\frac{\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu}{2\pi}\Bigl(\frac{\omega^{j}+\omega_{1}^{j}}{\lvert\omega+\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}}-\frac{\omega_{1}^{j}}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}}\Bigr),

which can be bounded in absolute value by

\left\lvert\frac{\omega^{j}+\omega_{1}^{j}}{\lvert\omega+\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}}-\frac{\omega_{1}^{j}}{\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}}\right\rvert=\frac{\lvert\omega^{j}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}-\omega_{1}^{j}\lvert\omega\rvert^{2}-\omega_{1}^{j}2\langle\omega,\omega_{1}\rangle\rvert}{\lvert\omega+\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{2}}\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega+\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}+\lvert\omega\rvert^{2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-1}\lvert\omega+\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}.

This yields the claim.
∎

We apply the following summation estimates repeatedly to establish the regularities of our diagrams.

Lemma C.2
.

It holds that uniformly in
\delta>0
,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\lvert k\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}1\lesssim(1\vee\delta^{-2})

(C.1)

and

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
\lvert k\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\lvert k\rvert^{-2}\lesssim(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1})).

(C.2)

What is more, for any
\alpha>2
,

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\lvert k\rvert^{-\alpha}\lesssim_{\alpha}1.

(C.3)

Proof.

The proof follows by treating the left-hand sides in (
C.1
)–(
C.3
) as Riemann sums and by bounding the associated Riemann integrals.
∎

We make repeated use of the following convolution estimate to construct non-linear objects.

Lemma C.3

(
[
ZZ15
, Lem. 3.10]
,
[
MWX17
, Lem. 5 & Lem. 6]
)
.

Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
\alpha+\beta>2
. We have uniformly in
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}\\
\omega_{1}\sim(\omega-\omega_{1})\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\alpha}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\beta}\lesssim_{\alpha+\beta}(1\vee\lvert\omega\rvert)^{-\alpha-\beta+2}.

If in addition
\alpha,\beta<2
, then we have uniformly in
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,

\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\alpha}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\beta}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta,\alpha+\beta}(1\vee\lvert\omega\rvert)^{-\alpha-\beta+2}.

The next convolution result is useful for estimating correlated frequencies
\omega\not=\omega^{\prime}
.

Lemma C.4
.

Let
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in(0,2)
be such that
\alpha+\gamma>2
and
\beta+\gamma>2
. Then for all
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
such that
\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}
it uniformly holds that

\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\alpha}\lvert\omega^{\prime}-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\beta}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\gamma}\lesssim\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-\beta}\lvert\omega\rvert^{-\alpha-\gamma+2}+\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-\alpha}\lvert\omega^{\prime}\rvert^{-\beta-\gamma+2}.

Proof.

The proof follows by two applications of Lemma
C.3
, one in the case
\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert\leq\lvert\omega-\omega^{\prime}\rvert/2
and the other in the complement.
∎

To derive finer estimates, it is useful to introduce a discrete paraproduct analogue to extend Lemma
C.3
, see (
1.9
) for the relevant notation.

Lemma C.5
.

Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
\alpha>2
and
\beta\geq 0
. We have uniformly in
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}\\
\omega_{1}\precsim(\omega-\omega_{1})\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\alpha}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-\beta}\lesssim_{\alpha}\lvert\omega\rvert^{-\beta}.

Proof.

The proof is immediate by (
C.3
) and the bound induced by (
1.9
).
∎

C.2
Double Sum Estimates

When we take the Fourier transform of the noise
\sigma\bm{\xi}
, it generates convolutions of
\widehat{\sigma}(t,\omega-m_{1})
against
\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j_{1}}(t,m_{1})
in
m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. We also generate convolutions in
\omega_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
by constructing non-linear objects such as
\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}\nabla\Phi_{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}}]
. Those steps lead to double sums over
\omega_{k}
and
m_{k}
that do not factorize. In this section, we estimate those sums.

We apply the following estimate in Subsection
2.5
to construct

.

Lemma C.6
.

Let
\gamma\in[0,1/2)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1-2\gamma)
. Then uniformly in
\omega,\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, it holds that

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1})\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{2\gamma}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}-m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{4}+m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\\
&\lesssim(1\vee\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-2+\varepsilon}(1\vee\lvert\omega\rvert)^{-1+2\gamma+\varepsilon}.\end{split}

Proof.

We decompose the sum over
m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
into the regions
m_{2}=\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}
,
m_{2}=-\omega_{4}
and
m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4},-\omega_{4}\}
.
We only give the bound that involves the sum over
m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4},-\omega_{4}\}
as the other ones are simpler. We estimate

\begin{split}&\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4},-\omega_{4}\}}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}-m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega_{4}+m_{2}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\\
&\leq\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4},-\omega_{4}\}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}-m_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{4}+m_{2}\rvert^{-2}.\end{split}

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
; we can then apply Lemma
C.3
to bound

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1})\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{2\gamma}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1}\\
&\quad\times\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4},-\omega_{4}\}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}-m_{2}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{4}+m_{2}\rvert^{-2}\\
&\lesssim(1\vee\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-2+2\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1})\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{2\gamma}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1}.\end{split}

Let
p\in(1,\infty)
,
q=p/(p-1)
and
\delta>0
. By Hölder’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1})\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{2\gamma}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1}\\
&\leq\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2p}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{\delta p}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1})^{p}\Bigr)^{1/p}\\
&\quad\times\Bigl(\sum_{\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-\delta q}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{2\gamma q}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-q}\Bigr)^{1/q}.\end{split}

We assume
\gamma\in[0,1/2)
,
2<p
and
1-2/p+2\gamma<\delta<2-2/p
. It follows that

p(2-\delta)>2,\qquad q(\delta-2\gamma)<2,\qquad q<2,\qquad q(\delta-2\gamma+1)>2.

and consequently by Lemma
C.3
,

\begin{split}&\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2p}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{\delta p}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1})^{p}\Bigr)^{1/p}\\
&\quad\times\Bigl(\sum_{\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-\delta q}\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{2\gamma q}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-q}\Bigr)^{1/q}\\
&\lesssim(1\vee\lvert\omega\rvert)^{-2+\delta+2/p}(1\vee\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-\delta+2\gamma-1+2/q}.\end{split}

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1-2\gamma)
; we can now set
\delta=1-2/p+2\gamma+\varepsilon
to conclude

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,\omega-\omega_{1}\}\\
(\omega-\omega_{4})\sim\omega_{4}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1})\lvert\omega_{4}\rvert^{2\gamma}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}-\omega_{4}\rvert^{-1}\lesssim(1\vee\lvert\omega\rvert)^{-1+2\gamma+\varepsilon}(1\vee\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert)^{-\varepsilon},

which yields the claim.
∎

We apply the following estimate in Subsection
2.6
to construct
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
,
{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}
and
{\raisebox{-3.01385pt}{\includegraphics[page,width]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}\!}^{\delta}
. We use the restriction
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}
induced by the cut-off
\varphi(\delta m_{1})
to establish a bound in terms of
\log(\delta^{-1})
.

Lemma C.7
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
and
\delta>0
. Then uniformly in
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
it holds that

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}(1+\lvert\omega\rvert\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-1})\\
&\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+3\varepsilon}(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1})).\end{split}

(C.4)

Proof.

To bound (
C.4
) it suffices to estimate the two parts

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}

(C.5)

and

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}\rvert^{-1}.

(C.6)

Let us consider (
C.5
). We decompose the sum over
m_{1}
into the regions
m_{1}=0
,
m_{1}=-\omega
and
m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}
.
The sum over
m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}
is given by

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}.

The sum over
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}
can then be further decomposed into the regions
\omega_{1}=m_{1}
,
\omega_{1}=\omega+m_{1}
and
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}
.
We only give the bound that involves the sums over
m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}
and
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}
. Using that
\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}
, we may estimate

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\\
&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}.\end{split}

Introducing the dyadic partition of unity
(\varrho_{q})_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}}
(cf. (
1.8
) & (
1.9
)), we decompose this sum into

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}\\
\omega_{1}\precsim(\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1})\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}

(C.7)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}\\
\omega_{1}\succsim(\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1})\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}

(C.8)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}\\
\omega_{1}\sim(\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1})\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}.

(C.9)

Assume
\varepsilon<2/3
; the terms (
C.7
) and (
C.9
) can be estimated by two applications of Lemma
C.3
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}\\
\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert\leq 64/9\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}\\
\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert\leq 64/9\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert m_{1}\rvert^{-2+2\varepsilon}\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+3\varepsilon}.\end{split}

The second term (
C.8
) can be estimated by Lemma
C.3
and (
C.2
),

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}\\
\omega_{1}\succsim(\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1})\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega,m_{1},\omega+m_{1}\}\\
\omega_{1}\succsim(\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1})\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\\
&\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+2\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,-\omega\}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\lvert\omega+m_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+3\varepsilon}(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1})).\end{split}

Decomposing (
C.5
) as discussed and bounding the resulting terms yields

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
\lvert m_{1}\rvert\leq\delta^{-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\omega_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0,\omega\}}(1+\lvert\omega_{1}-m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}(1+\lvert\omega-\omega_{1}+m_{1}\rvert^{2})^{-1}\lvert\omega_{1}\rvert^{-2}\lesssim\lvert\omega\rvert^{-2+3\varepsilon}(1\vee\log(\delta^{-1})).

Assume
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
; the bound on (
C.6
) then follows in a similar manner.
∎
